grasp baseline — editable region [READ-ONLY — reference implementation]

In causal-learn/bench/custom_algorithm.py:

python
Lines 3–158:
     1: import numpy as np
     2: from causallearn.graph.GeneralGraph import GeneralGraph
     3: from causallearn.graph.GraphNode import GraphNode
     4: 
     5: # =====================================================================
     6: # EDITABLE: implement run_causal_discovery below
     7: # =====================================================================
     8: class _GraspOrder:
     9:     def __init__(self, p, score):
    10:         self.order = list(range(p))
    11:         self.parents = {}
    12:         self.local_scores = {}
    13:         self.edges = 0
    14:         import random
    15:         random.shuffle(self.order)
    16:         for i in range(p):
    17:             y = self.order[i]
    18:             self.parents[y] = []
    19:             self.local_scores[y] = -score.score(y, [])
    20: 
    21:     def get(self, i): return self.order[i]
    22:     def set(self, i, y): self.order[i] = y
    23:     def index(self, y): return self.order.index(y)
    24:     def insert(self, i, y): self.order.insert(i, y)
    25:     def pop(self, i=-1): return self.order.pop(i)
    26:     def get_parents(self, y): return self.parents[y]
    27:     def set_parents(self, y, yp): self.parents[y] = yp
    28:     def get_local_score(self, y): return self.local_scores[y]
    29:     def set_local_score(self, y, s): self.local_scores[y] = s
    30:     def get_edges(self): return self.edges
    31:     def set_edges(self, e): self.edges = e
    32:     def bump_edges(self, b): self.edges += b
    33:     def len(self): return len(self.order)
    34: 
    35: 
    36: def _grasp_get_ancestors(y, ancestors, order):
    37:     ancestors.append(y)
    38:     for x in order.get_parents(y):
    39:         if x not in ancestors:
    40:             _grasp_get_ancestors(x, ancestors, order)
    41: 
    42: 
    43: def _grasp_tuck(i, j, order):
    44:     ancestors = []
    45:     _grasp_get_ancestors(order.get(i), ancestors, order)
    46:     shift = 0
    47:     for k in range(j + 1, i + 1):
    48:         if order.get(k) in ancestors:
    49:             order.insert(j + shift, order.pop(k))
    50:             shift += 1
    51: 
    52: 
    53: def _grasp_update(i, j, order, gsts):
    54:     edge_bump = 0
    55:     old_score = 0
    56:     new_score = 0
    57:     for k in range(j, i + 1):
    58:         z = order.get(k)
    59:         z_parents = order.get_parents(z)
    60:         edge_bump -= len(z_parents)
    61:         old_score += order.get_local_score(z)
    62:         z_parents.clear()
    63:         candidates = [order.get(l) for l in range(0, k)]
    64:         local_score = gsts[z].trace(candidates, z_parents)
    65:         order.set_local_score(z, local_score)
    66:         edge_bump += len(z_parents)
    67:         new_score += local_score
    68:     return edge_bump, new_score - old_score
    69: 
    70: 
    71: def _grasp_dfs(depth, flipped, history, order, gsts):
    72:     import random
    73:     cache = [{}, {}, {}, 0]
    74:     indices = list(range(order.len()))
    75:     random.shuffle(indices)
    76:     for i in indices:
    77:         y = order.get(i)
    78:         y_parents = order.get_parents(y)
    79:         random.shuffle(y_parents)
    80:         for x in y_parents:
    81:             covered = set([x] + order.get_parents(x)) == set(y_parents)
    82:             if len(history) > 0 and not covered:
    83:                 continue
    84:             j = order.index(x)
    85:             for k in range(j, i + 1):
    86:                 z = order.get(k)
    87:                 cache[0][k] = z
    88:                 cache[1][k] = order.get_parents(z)[:]
    89:                 cache[2][k] = order.get_local_score(z)
    90:             cache[3] = order.get_edges()
    91:             _grasp_tuck(i, j, order)
    92:             edge_bump, score_bump = _grasp_update(i, j, order, gsts)
    93:             if score_bump > 1e-6:
    94:                 order.bump_edges(edge_bump)
    95:                 return True
    96:             if score_bump > -1e-6:
    97:                 flipped = flipped ^ set(
    98:                     [
    99:                         tuple(sorted([x, z]))
   100:                         for z in order.get_parents(x)
   101:                         if order.index(z) < i
   102:                     ]
   103:                 )
   104:                 if len(flipped) > 0 and flipped not in history:
   105:                     history.append(flipped)
   106:                     if depth > 0 and _grasp_dfs(depth - 1, flipped, history, order, gsts):
   107:                         return True
   108:                     del history[-1]
   109:             for k in range(j, i + 1):
   110:                 z = cache[0][k]
   111:                 order.set(k, z)
   112:                 order.set_parents(z, cache[1][k])
   113:                 order.set_local_score(z, cache[2][k])
   114:             order.set_edges(cache[3])
   115:     return False
   116: 
   117: 
   118: def run_causal_discovery(X: np.ndarray) -> GeneralGraph:
   119:     """
   120:     Input:  X of shape (n_samples, n_variables)
   121:     Output: estimated CPDAG as causallearn.graph.GeneralGraph.GeneralGraph
   122:     """
   123:     from causallearn.score.LocalScoreFunctionClass import LocalScoreClass
   124:     from causallearn.score.LocalScoreFunction import local_score_BIC_from_cov
   125:     from causallearn.search.PermutationBased.gst import GST
   126:     from causallearn.utils.DAG2CPDAG import dag2cpdag
   127: 
   128:     X = X.copy()
   129:     n, p = X.shape
   130:     depth = 3
   131:     parameters = {"lambda_value": 2}
   132:     score = LocalScoreClass(
   133:         data=X, local_score_fun=local_score_BIC_from_cov, parameters=parameters
   134:     )
   135:     gsts = [GST(i, score) for i in range(p)]
   136: 
   137:     nodes = [GraphNode("X%d" % (i + 1)) for i in range(p)]
   138:     G = GeneralGraph(nodes)
   139: 
   140:     order = _GraspOrder(p, score)
   141: 
   142:     for i in range(p):
   143:         y = order.get(i)
   144:         y_parents = order.get_parents(y)
   145:         candidates = [order.get(j) for j in range(0, i)]
   146:         local_score = gsts[y].trace(candidates, y_parents)
   147:         order.set_local_score(y, local_score)
   148:         order.bump_edges(len(y_parents))
   149: 
   150:     while _grasp_dfs(depth - 1, set(), [], order, gsts):
   151:         pass
   152: 
   153:     for y in range(p):
   154:         for x in order.get_parents(y):
   155:             G.add_directed_edge(nodes[x], nodes[y])
   156: 
   157:     G = dag2cpdag(G)
   158:     return G
   159: # =====================================================================

boss baseline — editable region [READ-ONLY — reference implementation]

In causal-learn/bench/custom_algorithm.py:

python
Lines 3–92:
     1: import numpy as np
     2: from causallearn.graph.GeneralGraph import GeneralGraph
     3: from causallearn.graph.GraphNode import GraphNode
     4: 
     5: # =====================================================================
     6: # EDITABLE: implement run_causal_discovery below
     7: # =====================================================================
     8: def _boss_reversed_enumerate(iter_, j):
     9:     for w in reversed(iter_):
    10:         yield j, w
    11:         j -= 1
    12: 
    13: 
    14: def _boss_better_mutation(v, order, gsts):
    15:     i = order.index(v)
    16:     p = len(order)
    17:     scores = np.zeros(p + 1)
    18: 
    19:     prefix = []
    20:     score = 0
    21:     for j, w in enumerate(order):
    22:         scores[j] = gsts[v].trace(prefix) + score
    23:         if v != w:
    24:             score += gsts[w].trace(prefix)
    25:             prefix.append(w)
    26: 
    27:     scores[p] = gsts[v].trace(prefix) + score
    28:     best = p
    29: 
    30:     prefix.append(v)
    31:     score = 0
    32:     for j, w in _boss_reversed_enumerate(order, p - 1):
    33:         if v != w:
    34:             prefix.remove(w)
    35:             score += gsts[w].trace(prefix)
    36:         scores[j] += score
    37:         if scores[j] > scores[best]:
    38:             best = j
    39: 
    40:     if scores[i] + 1e-6 > scores[best]:
    41:         return False
    42:     order.remove(v)
    43:     order.insert(best - int(best > i), v)
    44:     return True
    45: 
    46: 
    47: def run_causal_discovery(X: np.ndarray) -> GeneralGraph:
    48:     """
    49:     Input:  X of shape (n_samples, n_variables)
    50:     Output: estimated CPDAG as causallearn.graph.GeneralGraph.GeneralGraph
    51:     """
    52:     import random
    53:     import sys
    54:     import time
    55:     from causallearn.score.LocalScoreFunctionClass import LocalScoreClass
    56:     from causallearn.score.LocalScoreFunction import local_score_BIC_from_cov
    57:     from causallearn.search.PermutationBased.gst import GST
    58:     from causallearn.utils.DAG2CPDAG import dag2cpdag
    59: 
    60:     X = X.copy()
    61:     n, p = X.shape
    62:     parameters = {"lambda_value": 2}
    63:     score = LocalScoreClass(
    64:         data=X, local_score_fun=local_score_BIC_from_cov, parameters=parameters
    65:     )
    66: 
    67:     nodes = [GraphNode("X%d" % (i + 1)) for i in range(p)]
    68:     G = GeneralGraph(nodes)
    69: 
    70:     order = list(range(p))
    71:     gsts = [GST(v, score) for v in order]
    72:     parents = {v: [] for v in order}
    73: 
    74:     variables = list(order)
    75:     while True:
    76:         improved = False
    77:         random.shuffle(variables)
    78:         for v in variables:
    79:             improved |= _boss_better_mutation(v, order, gsts)
    80:         if not improved:
    81:             break
    82: 
    83:     for i, v in enumerate(order):
    84:         parents[v].clear()
    85:         gsts[v].trace(order[:i], parents[v])
    86: 
    87:     for y in range(p):
    88:         for x in parents[y]:
    89:             G.add_directed_edge(nodes[x], nodes[y])
    90: 
    91:     G = dag2cpdag(G)
    92:     return G
    93: # =====================================================================

Tips

Keep the function/class signatures of the editable regions identical;
evaluation imports them by name.
Determinism matters: seeds are fixed; don't introduce hidden randomness.
The baseline implementations above are deliberately strong. Aim for an
algorithmic improvement — many hyperparameters are locked outside the editable surface anyway.

Time Budget

You have 5 hours of wall-clock time before submission, covering
everything you do here: reading the code, editing it, and any trial runs
you launch.

Good luck.

📄 §5.3 causal-observational-linear-non-gaussian — 模型看到的主文档：中文翻译 + 英文原文（均默认折叠）
🇨🇳 [中文翻译] Causal Observational (Linear Non-Gaussian)

MLS-Bench: causal-observational-linear-non-gaussian

因果发现：观测性线性非高斯数据（LiNGAM）

研究问题

实现一个因果发现算法，从由线性非高斯无环模型（LiNGAM）生成的纯观测数据中恢复有向 DAG。

背景

基于 LiNGAM 的方法利用非高斯噪声，仅凭观测数据即可实现完整的 DAG 可辨识性，从而超越了基于约束（PC）与基于评分（GES）方法的马尔可夫等价类限制。这一原始可辨识性结果出自 Shimizu、Hoyer、Hyvarinen 与 Kerminen 的 "A Linear Non-Gaussian Acyclic Model for Causal Discovery"（JMLR 7, 2006）。

该方法应能处理中等至较大规模的变量数量，并避免假设单一固定的噪声分布或图族。数据由带有符号线性边权重和独立非高斯噪声的合成 DAG 生成。

任务

在 causal-learn/bench/custom_algorithm.py 中实现 run_causal_discovery(X)。它必须返回一个与基准评估兼容的有向 DAG（骨架 + 正确的边方向）。

参考基线

icalingam: 基于 ICA 的 LiNGAM。Shimizu、Hoyer、Hyvarinen 与 Kerminen，"A Linear Non-Gaussian Acyclic Model for Causal Discovery"，JMLR 7, 2006。
directlingam: DirectLiNGAM。Shimizu 等，"DirectLiNGAM: A Direct Method for Learning a Linear Non-Gaussian Structural Equation Model"，JMLR 12, 2011（arXiv:1101.2489）。
notears: NOTEARS。Zheng、Aragam、Ravikumar 与 Xing，"DAGs with NO TEARS: Continuous Optimization for Structure Learning"，NeurIPS 2018（arXiv:1803.01422）。借助平滑无环约束进行连续的基于评分的 DAG 学习。

科学贡献应当是一种利用非高斯观测结构的模块化方法，例如独立分量思想、直接因果排序、连续优化、混合筛选，或一种新的有向图恢复流程。

你的工作区

你在 /workspace 中工作。包源码树 /workspace/causal-learn/ 是本任务的研究脚手架。

你可以编辑的文件

你只能修改这些文件，并且只能在列出的行范围内（含端点，行号从 1 开始计数）修改。改动这些范围之外代码的编辑——或者创建新文件、或者删除整个文件——都会导致你的提交无效。

行号标记的是一个可编辑的区域，而不是固定的行数预算：你可以在其中增加或删除行。只有可编辑范围之外的代码必须保持不变。

icalingam 基线 — 可编辑区域 [只读 — 参考实现]

在 causal-learn/bench/custom_algorithm.py 中：

python
Lines 3–77:
     1: import numpy as np
     2: 
     3: # =====================================================================
     4: # EDITABLE: implement run_causal_discovery below
     5: # =====================================================================
     6: def run_causal_discovery(X: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
     7:     """
     8:     Input:  X of shape (n_samples, n_variables)
     9:     Output: adjacency matrix B of shape (n_variables, n_variables)
    10:             B[i, j] != 0  means j -> i  (follows causal-learn convention)
    11:     """
    12:     import os
    13:     import numpy as np
    14:     from scipy.optimize import linear_sum_assignment
    15:     from sklearn.decomposition import FastICA
    16:     from sklearn.utils import check_array
    17:     from causallearn.search.FCMBased.lingam.base import _BaseLiNGAM
    18: 
    19:     X = check_array(X)
    20:     seed = int(os.environ.get("SEED", "42"))
    21: 
    22:     # Core ICALiNGAM steps (from causallearn.search.FCMBased.lingam.ica_lingam)
    23:     ica = FastICA(max_iter=1000, random_state=seed)
    24:     ica.fit(X)
    25:     W_ica = ica.components_
    26: 
    27:     _, col_index = linear_sum_assignment(1 / np.abs(W_ica))
    28:     PW_ica = np.zeros_like(W_ica)
    29:     PW_ica[col_index] = W_ica
    30: 
    31:     D = np.diag(PW_ica)[:, np.newaxis]
    32:     W_estimate = PW_ica / D
    33:     B_estimate = np.eye(len(W_estimate)) - W_estimate
    34: 
    35:     def _search_causal_order(matrix: np.ndarray):
    36:         causal_order = []
    37:         row_num = matrix.shape[0]
    38:         original_index = np.arange(row_num)
    39:         while 0 < len(matrix):
    40:             row_index_list = np.where(np.sum(np.abs(matrix), axis=1) == 0)[0]
    41:             if len(row_index_list) == 0:
    42:                 break
    43:             target_index = row_index_list[0]
    44:             causal_order.append(original_index[target_index])
    45:             original_index = np.delete(original_index, target_index, axis=0)
    46:             mask = np.delete(np.arange(len(matrix)), target_index, axis=0)
    47:             matrix = matrix[mask][:, mask]
    48:         if len(causal_order) != row_num:
    49:             return None
    50:         return causal_order
    51: 
    52:     def _estimate_causal_order(matrix: np.ndarray):
    53:         matrix = matrix.copy()
    54:         pos_list = np.argsort(np.abs(matrix), axis=None)
    55:         pos_list = np.vstack(np.unravel_index(pos_list, matrix.shape)).T
    56:         initial_zero_num = int(matrix.shape[0] * (matrix.shape[0] + 1) / 2)
    57:         for i, j in pos_list[:initial_zero_num]:
    58:             matrix[i, j] = 0
    59:         causal_order = None
    60:         for i, j in pos_list[initial_zero_num:]:
    61:             matrix[i, j] = 0
    62:             causal_order = _search_causal_order(matrix)
    63:             if causal_order is not None:
    64:                 break
    65:         return causal_order
    66: 
    67:     causal_order = _estimate_causal_order(B_estimate)
    68: 
    69:     class _LocalICALiNGAM(_BaseLiNGAM):
    70:         def fit(self, X):
    71:             return self
    72: 
    73:     model = _LocalICALiNGAM(random_state=seed)
    74:     model._causal_order = causal_order
    75:     model._estimate_adjacency_matrix(X)
    76:     return model.adjacency_matrix_
    77: # =====================================================================

directlingam 基线 — 可编辑区域 [只读 — 参考实现]

在 causal-learn/bench/custom_algorithm.py 中：

python
Lines 3–75:
     1: import numpy as np
     2: 
     3: # =====================================================================
     4: # EDITABLE: implement run_causal_discovery below
     5: # =====================================================================
     6: def run_causal_discovery(X: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
     7:     """
     8:     Input:  X of shape (n_samples, n_variables)
     9:     Output: adjacency matrix B of shape (n_variables, n_variables)
    10:             B[i, j] != 0  means j -> i  (follows causal-learn convention)
    11:     """
    12:     import os
    13:     import numpy as np
    14:     from sklearn.utils import check_array
    15:     from causallearn.search.FCMBased.lingam.base import _BaseLiNGAM
    16: 
    17:     X = check_array(X)
    18:     seed = int(os.environ.get("SEED", "42"))
    19:     n_features = X.shape[1]
    20: 
    21:     # Core DirectLiNGAM steps (from causallearn.search.FCMBased.lingam.direct_lingam)
    22:     def _residual(xi: np.ndarray, xj: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    23:         return xi - (np.cov(xi, xj)[0, 1] / np.var(xj)) * xj
    24: 
    25:     def _entropy(u: np.ndarray) -> float:
    26:         k1 = 79.047
    27:         k2 = 7.4129
    28:         gamma = 0.37457
    29:         return (1 + np.log(2 * np.pi)) / 2 -                k1 * (np.mean(np.log(np.cosh(u))) - gamma) ** 2 -                k2 * (np.mean(u * np.exp((-u ** 2) / 2))) ** 2
    30: 
    31:     def _diff_mutual_info(
    32:         xi_std: np.ndarray,
    33:         xj_std: np.ndarray,
    34:         ri_j: np.ndarray,
    35:         rj_i: np.ndarray,
    36:     ) -> float:
    37:         return (_entropy(xj_std) + _entropy(ri_j / np.std(ri_j))) -                (_entropy(xi_std) + _entropy(rj_i / np.std(rj_i)))
    38: 
    39:     def _search_causal_order(X_work: np.ndarray, U: np.ndarray) -> int:
    40:         if len(U) == 1:
    41:             return int(U[0])
    42:         M_list = []
    43:         for i in U:
    44:             M = 0.0
    45:             for j in U:
    46:                 if i == j:
    47:                     continue
    48:                 xi_std = (X_work[:, i] - np.mean(X_work[:, i])) / np.std(X_work[:, i])
    49:                 xj_std = (X_work[:, j] - np.mean(X_work[:, j])) / np.std(X_work[:, j])
    50:                 ri_j = _residual(xi_std, xj_std)
    51:                 rj_i = _residual(xj_std, xi_std)
    52:                 M += np.min([0.0, _diff_mutual_info(xi_std, xj_std, ri_j, rj_i)]) ** 2
    53:             M_list.append(-1.0 * M)
    54:         return int(U[np.argmax(M_list)])
    55: 
    56:     U = np.arange(n_features)
    57:     K = []
    58:     X_work = np.copy(X)
    59:     for _ in range(n_features):
    60:         m = _search_causal_order(X_work, U)
    61:         for i in U:
    62:             if i != m:
    63:                 X_work[:, i] = _residual(X_work[:, i], X_work[:, m])
    64:         K.append(m)
    65:         U = U[U != m]
    66: 
    67:     class _LocalDirectLiNGAM(_BaseLiNGAM):
    68:         def fit(self, X):
    69:             return self
    70: 
    71:     model = _LocalDirectLiNGAM(random_state=seed)
    72:     model._causal_order = K
    73:     model._estimate_adjacency_matrix(X)
    74:     return model.adjacency_matrix_
    75: # =====================================================================

notears 基线 — 可编辑区域 [只读 — 参考实现]

在 causal-learn/bench/custom_algorithm.py 中：

python
Lines 3–83:
     1: import numpy as np
     2: 
     3: # =====================================================================
     4: # EDITABLE: implement run_causal_discovery below
     5: # =====================================================================
     6: def run_causal_discovery(X: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
     7:     """
     8:     Input:  X of shape (n_samples, n_variables)
     9:     Output: adjacency matrix B of shape (n_variables, n_variables)
    10:             B[i, j] != 0  means j -> i  (follows causal-learn convention)
    11:     """
    12:     import numpy as np
    13:     import scipy.linalg as sla
    14:     from scipy.optimize import minimize
    15:     from sklearn.utils import check_array
    16: 
    17:     X = check_array(X)
    18:     n, d = X.shape
    19: 
    20:     # Reference defaults from Zheng et al. 2018 reference impl.
    21:     lambda1 = 0.1
    22:     max_iter = 100
    23:     h_tol = 1e-8
    24:     rho_max = 1e16
    25:     w_threshold = 0.3
    26: 
    27:     def _loss_and_grad(W):
    28:         # Squared-error regression loss: 1/(2n) * ||X - X W||^2
    29:         R = X - X @ W
    30:         loss = 0.5 / n * (R ** 2).sum()
    31:         G = -1.0 / n * X.T @ R
    32:         return loss, G
    33: 
    34:     def _h_and_grad(W):
    35:         # h(W) = tr(exp(W*W)) - d  (Zheng 2018 smooth acyclicity)
    36:         M = W * W
    37:         E = sla.expm(M)
    38:         h = np.trace(E) - d
    39:         G = E.T * 2 * W
    40:         return h, G
    41: 
    42:     def _obj(w_pm, rho, alpha):
    43:         w_pm = w_pm.reshape(2, d * d)
    44:         W = (w_pm[0] - w_pm[1]).reshape(d, d)
    45:         loss, G_loss = _loss_and_grad(W)
    46:         h, G_h = _h_and_grad(W)
    47:         obj = loss + 0.5 * rho * h * h + alpha * h + lambda1 * w_pm.sum()
    48:         G_smooth = G_loss + (rho * h + alpha) * G_h
    49:         g = np.concatenate([
    50:             (G_smooth + lambda1).flatten(),
    51:             (-G_smooth + lambda1).flatten(),
    52:         ])
    53:         return obj, g
    54: 
    55:     w_est = np.zeros(2 * d * d)
    56:     rho, alpha, h = 1.0, 0.0, np.inf
    57:     # Non-negative bounds; force diagonal to zero (no self-loops)
    58:     bnds = [(0, 0) if (i == j) else (0, None)
    59:             for _ in range(2) for i in range(d) for j in range(d)]
    60: 
    61:     for _ in range(max_iter):
    62:         while rho < rho_max:
    63:             sol = minimize(_obj, w_est, args=(rho, alpha),
    64:                            method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, bounds=bnds)
    65:             w_new = sol.x
    66:             W_new = (w_new[:d * d] - w_new[d * d:]).reshape(d, d)
    67:             h_new, _ = _h_and_grad(W_new)
    68:             if h_new > 0.25 * h:
    69:                 rho *= 10
    70:             else:
    71:                 break
    72:         w_est, h = w_new, h_new
    73:         alpha += rho * h
    74:         if h <= h_tol or rho >= rho_max:
    75:             break
    76: 
    77:     W_final = (w_est[:d * d] - w_est[d * d:]).reshape(d, d)
    78:     W_final[np.abs(W_final) < w_threshold] = 0.0
    79: 
    80:     # NOTEARS: W[i, j] != 0 means i -> j
    81:     # causal-learn / this task: B[i, j] != 0 means j -> i. Transpose.
    82:     return W_final.T
    83: # =====================================================================

🇬🇧 [English Original] Causal Observational (Linear Non-Gaussian)

MLS-Bench: causal-observational-linear-non-gaussian

Causal Discovery: Observational Linear Non-Gaussian Data (LiNGAM)

Research Question

Implement a causal discovery algorithm that recovers the directed DAG from purely observational data generated by a Linear Non-Gaussian Acyclic Model (LiNGAM).

Background

LiNGAM-based methods exploit non-Gaussian noise to achieve full DAG identifiability from observational data alone, going beyond the Markov Equivalence Class limit of constraint-based (PC) and score-based (GES) methods. The original identifiability result is due to Shimizu, Hoyer, Hyvarinen & Kerminen, "A Linear Non-Gaussian Acyclic Model for Causal Discovery," JMLR 7, 2006.

The method should handle moderate-to-large variable counts and avoid assuming a single fixed noise distribution or graph family. Data is generated from synthetic DAGs with signed linear edge weights and independent non-Gaussian noise.

Task

Implement run_causal_discovery(X) in causal-learn/bench/custom_algorithm.py. It must return a directed DAG (skeleton + correct edge orientation) compatible with the benchmark evaluation.

Reference baselines

icalingam: ICA-based LiNGAM. Shimizu, Hoyer, Hyvarinen & Kerminen,
"A Linear Non-Gaussian Acyclic Model for Causal Discovery," JMLR 7, 2006.
directlingam: DirectLiNGAM. Shimizu et al., "DirectLiNGAM: A Direct Method
for Learning a Linear Non-Gaussian Structural Equation Model," JMLR 12, 2011 (arXiv:1101.2489).
notears: NOTEARS. Zheng, Aragam, Ravikumar & Xing, "DAGs with NO TEARS:
Continuous Optimization for Structure Learning," NeurIPS 2018 (arXiv:1803.01422). Continuous score-based DAG learning via a smooth acyclicity constraint.

The scientific contribution should be a modular method for exploiting
non-Gaussian observational structure, such as independent component ideas,
direct causal ordering, continuous optimization, hybrid screening, or a new
directed-graph recovery procedure.

Your Workspace

You are working inside /workspace. The package source tree
/workspace/causal-learn/ is the research scaffold for this task.

icalingam baseline — editable region [READ-ONLY — reference implementation]

In causal-learn/bench/custom_algorithm.py:

python
Lines 3–77:
     1: import numpy as np
     2: 
     3: # =====================================================================
     4: # EDITABLE: implement run_causal_discovery below
     5: # =====================================================================
     6: def run_causal_discovery(X: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
     7:     """
     8:     Input:  X of shape (n_samples, n_variables)
     9:     Output: adjacency matrix B of shape (n_variables, n_variables)
    10:             B[i, j] != 0  means j -> i  (follows causal-learn convention)
    11:     """
    12:     import os
    13:     import numpy as np
    14:     from scipy.optimize import linear_sum_assignment
    15:     from sklearn.decomposition import FastICA
    16:     from sklearn.utils import check_array
    17:     from causallearn.search.FCMBased.lingam.base import _BaseLiNGAM
    18: 
    19:     X = check_array(X)
    20:     seed = int(os.environ.get("SEED", "42"))
    21: 
    22:     # Core ICALiNGAM steps (from causallearn.search.FCMBased.lingam.ica_lingam)
    23:     ica = FastICA(max_iter=1000, random_state=seed)
    24:     ica.fit(X)
    25:     W_ica = ica.components_
    26: 
    27:     _, col_index = linear_sum_assignment(1 / np.abs(W_ica))
    28:     PW_ica = np.zeros_like(W_ica)
    29:     PW_ica[col_index] = W_ica
    30: 
    31:     D = np.diag(PW_ica)[:, np.newaxis]
    32:     W_estimate = PW_ica / D
    33:     B_estimate = np.eye(len(W_estimate)) - W_estimate
    34: 
    35:     def _search_causal_order(matrix: np.ndarray):
    36:         causal_order = []
    37:         row_num = matrix.shape[0]
    38:         original_index = np.arange(row_num)
    39:         while 0 < len(matrix):
    40:             row_index_list = np.where(np.sum(np.abs(matrix), axis=1) == 0)[0]
    41:             if len(row_index_list) == 0:
    42:                 break
    43:             target_index = row_index_list[0]
    44:             causal_order.append(original_index[target_index])
    45:             original_index = np.delete(original_index, target_index, axis=0)
    46:             mask = np.delete(np.arange(len(matrix)), target_index, axis=0)
    47:             matrix = matrix[mask][:, mask]
    48:         if len(causal_order) != row_num:
    49:             return None
    50:         return causal_order
    51: 
    52:     def _estimate_causal_order(matrix: np.ndarray):
    53:         matrix = matrix.copy()
    54:         pos_list = np.argsort(np.abs(matrix), axis=None)
    55:         pos_list = np.vstack(np.unravel_index(pos_list, matrix.shape)).T
    56:         initial_zero_num = int(matrix.shape[0] * (matrix.shape[0] + 1) / 2)
    57:         for i, j in pos_list[:initial_zero_num]:
    58:             matrix[i, j] = 0
    59:         causal_order = None
    60:         for i, j in pos_list[initial_zero_num:]:
    61:             matrix[i, j] = 0
    62:             causal_order = _search_causal_order(matrix)
    63:             if causal_order is not None:
    64:                 break
    65:         return causal_order
    66: 
    67:     causal_order = _estimate_causal_order(B_estimate)
    68: 
    69:     class _LocalICALiNGAM(_BaseLiNGAM):
    70:         def fit(self, X):
    71:             return self
    72: 
    73:     model = _LocalICALiNGAM(random_state=seed)
    74:     model._causal_order = causal_order
    75:     model._estimate_adjacency_matrix(X)
    76:     return model.adjacency_matrix_
    77: # =====================================================================

directlingam baseline — editable region [READ-ONLY — reference implementation]

In causal-learn/bench/custom_algorithm.py:

python
Lines 3–75:
     1: import numpy as np
     2: 
     3: # =====================================================================
     4: # EDITABLE: implement run_causal_discovery below
     5: # =====================================================================
     6: def run_causal_discovery(X: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
     7:     """
     8:     Input:  X of shape (n_samples, n_variables)
     9:     Output: adjacency matrix B of shape (n_variables, n_variables)
    10:             B[i, j] != 0  means j -> i  (follows causal-learn convention)
    11:     """
    12:     import os
    13:     import numpy as np
    14:     from sklearn.utils import check_array
    15:     from causallearn.search.FCMBased.lingam.base import _BaseLiNGAM
    16: 
    17:     X = check_array(X)
    18:     seed = int(os.environ.get("SEED", "42"))
    19:     n_features = X.shape[1]
    20: 
    21:     # Core DirectLiNGAM steps (from causallearn.search.FCMBased.lingam.direct_lingam)
    22:     def _residual(xi: np.ndarray, xj: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    23:         return xi - (np.cov(xi, xj)[0, 1] / np.var(xj)) * xj
    24: 
    25:     def _entropy(u: np.ndarray) -> float:
    26:         k1 = 79.047
    27:         k2 = 7.4129
    28:         gamma = 0.37457
    29:         return (1 + np.log(2 * np.pi)) / 2 -                k1 * (np.mean(np.log(np.cosh(u))) - gamma) ** 2 -                k2 * (np.mean(u * np.exp((-u ** 2) / 2))) ** 2
    30: 
    31:     def _diff_mutual_info(
    32:         xi_std: np.ndarray,
    33:         xj_std: np.ndarray,
    34:         ri_j: np.ndarray,
    35:         rj_i: np.ndarray,
    36:     ) -> float:
    37:         return (_entropy(xj_std) + _entropy(ri_j / np.std(ri_j))) -                (_entropy(xi_std) + _entropy(rj_i / np.std(rj_i)))
    38: 
    39:     def _search_causal_order(X_work: np.ndarray, U: np.ndarray) -> int:
    40:         if len(U) == 1:
    41:             return int(U[0])
    42:         M_list = []
    43:         for i in U:
    44:             M = 0.0
    45:             for j in U:
    46:                 if i == j:
    47:                     continue
    48:                 xi_std = (X_work[:, i] - np.mean(X_work[:, i])) / np.std(X_work[:, i])
    49:                 xj_std = (X_work[:, j] - np.mean(X_work[:, j])) / np.std(X_work[:, j])
    50:                 ri_j = _residual(xi_std, xj_std)
    51:                 rj_i = _residual(xj_std, xi_std)
    52:                 M += np.min([0.0, _diff_mutual_info(xi_std, xj_std, ri_j, rj_i)]) ** 2
    53:             M_list.append(-1.0 * M)
    54:         return int(U[np.argmax(M_list)])
    55: 
    56:     U = np.arange(n_features)
    57:     K = []
    58:     X_work = np.copy(X)
    59:     for _ in range(n_features):
    60:         m = _search_causal_order(X_work, U)
    61:         for i in U:
    62:             if i != m:
    63:                 X_work[:, i] = _residual(X_work[:, i], X_work[:, m])
    64:         K.append(m)
    65:         U = U[U != m]
    66: 
    67:     class _LocalDirectLiNGAM(_BaseLiNGAM):
    68:         def fit(self, X):
    69:             return self
    70: 
    71:     model = _LocalDirectLiNGAM(random_state=seed)
    72:     model._causal_order = K
    73:     model._estimate_adjacency_matrix(X)
    74:     return model.adjacency_matrix_
    75: # =====================================================================

    79: 
    80:     # NOTEARS: W[i, j] != 0 means i -> j
    81:     # causal-learn / this task: B[i, j] != 0 means j -> i. Transpose.

📄 §5.4 causal-observational-nonlinear — 模型看到的主文档：中文翻译 + 英文原文（均默认折叠）
🇨🇳 [中文翻译] Causal Observational (Nonlinear)

MLS-Bench: causal-observational-nonlinear

因果发现：观测性非线性数据（加性噪声模型）

研究问题

实现一个因果发现算法，从由非线性加性噪声模型（ANM）生成的纯观测数据中恢复有向 DAG。

背景

加性噪声模型（Additive Noise Models）假设结构方程形如 x_j = f_j(parents(j)) + e_j，其中 f_j 是非线性函数，e_j 是相互独立的噪声项。在对 f_j 和噪声分布的温和条件下，因果 DAG 仅凭观测数据即可辨识（Hoyer、Janzing、Mooij、Peters 与 Scholkopf，"Nonlinear causal discovery with additive noise models"，NeurIPS 2008；Peters、Mooij、Janzing 与 Scholkopf，"Causal Discovery with Continuous Additive Noise Models"，JMLR 15, 2014）。这超越了假设线性关系的线性方法（LiNGAM、PC、GES）。

关键挑战包括：(1) 在高维非线性场景中进行独立性检验，(2) 扩展到更大的图，(3) 处理多样的非线性函数类型（MLP、GP、多项式、sigmoid），以及 (4) 在噪声为高斯时的鲁棒性——此时 ANM 的可辨识性变得更加微妙。

任务

在 causal-learn/bench/custom_algorithm.py 中实现 run_causal_discovery(X)。它必须返回一个与基准评估兼容的有向 DAG。

参考基线

cam: 结合 GAM 回归的因果加性模型（Causal Additive Models）。基于评分的方法。Buhlmann、Peters 与 Ernest，"CAM: Causal additive models, high-dimensional order search and penalized regression"，Annals of Statistics 42(6), 2014（arXiv:1310.1533）。
notears_mlp: 采用 MLP 参数化来刻画非线性加性结构的 NOTEARS。连续优化。Zheng、Dan、Aragam、Ravikumar 与 Xing，"Learning Sparse Nonparametric DAGs"，AISTATS 2020（arXiv:1909.13189）。默认设置与论文一致，使用带 sigmoid 激活的单隐层 MLP。
directlingam: 线性参考基线。Shimizu 等，JMLR 12, 2011（arXiv:1101.2489）。
grandag: GraN-DAG。Lachapelle、Brouillard、Deleu 与 Lacoste-Julien，"Gradient-Based Neural DAG Learning"，ICLR 2020（arXiv:1906.02226）。基于梯度的 DAG 学习，使用神经条件密度并以增广拉格朗日无环约束加以约束。

合适的贡献包括非线性独立性检验、加性噪声评分、可微 DAG 学习、混合排序方法，或其他在所评估图规模下仍保持实用的、能够利用非线性因果不对称性的模块化流程。

你的工作区

你在 /workspace 中工作。包源码树 /workspace/causal-learn/ 是本任务的研究脚手架。

行号标记的是一个可编辑的区域，而不是固定的行数预算：你可以在其中增加或删除行。只有可编辑范围之外的代码必须保持不变。

python
Lines 3–113:
     1: import numpy as np
     2: 
     3: # =====================================================================
     4: # EDITABLE: implement run_causal_discovery below
     5: # =====================================================================
     6: def run_causal_discovery(X: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
     7:     """
     8:     Input:  X of shape (n_samples, n_variables)
     9:     Output: adjacency matrix B of shape (n_variables, n_variables)
    10:             B[i, j] != 0  means j -> i  (follows causal-learn convention)
    11:     """
    12:     import os
    13:     import numpy as np
    14:     from sklearn.ensemble import GradientBoostingRegressor
    15:     from sklearn.model_selection import cross_val_score
    16: 
    17:     n_samples, n_vars = X.shape
    18:     seed = int(os.environ.get("SEED", "42"))
    19: 
    20:     # --- Step 1: Estimate causal ordering via greedy score-based search ---
    21:     # For each variable, compute residual variance after nonlinear regression
    22:     # on candidate parents. Variables with lower residual variance given
    23:     # earlier variables are placed later in the ordering.
    24: 
    25:     remaining = list(range(n_vars))
    26:     ordering = []
    27: 
    28:     while remaining:
    29:         if len(ordering) == 0:
    30:             # First variable: pick the one with highest marginal variance
    31:             # (root causes tend to have variance = noise variance only)
    32:             scores = []
    33:             for j in remaining:
    34:                 scores.append(np.var(X[:, j]))
    35:             # Pick the one with smallest variance (likely a root)
    36:             best_idx = np.argmin(scores)
    37:             ordering.append(remaining.pop(best_idx))
    38:         else:
    39:             # For each remaining var, fit nonlinear regression on current ordering
    40:             best_score = np.inf
    41:             best_var = None
    42:             best_var_idx = None
    43:             parents_X = X[:, ordering]
    44:             for idx, j in enumerate(remaining):
    45:                 y = X[:, j]
    46:                 # Use gradient boosting as nonlinear regressor
    47:                 gbr = GradientBoostingRegressor(
    48:                     n_estimators=50, max_depth=3, learning_rate=0.1,
    49:                     random_state=seed, subsample=0.8
    50:                 )
    51:                 gbr.fit(parents_X, y)
    52:                 residuals = y - gbr.predict(parents_X)
    53:                 resid_var = np.var(residuals)
    54:                 if resid_var < best_score:
    55:                     best_score = resid_var
    56:                     best_var = j
    57:                     best_var_idx = idx
    58:             ordering.append(remaining.pop(best_var_idx))
    59: 
    60:     # --- Step 2: Preliminary adjacency via nonlinear regression along ordering ---
    61:     B = np.zeros((n_vars, n_vars))
    62:     for pos in range(1, len(ordering)):
    63:         j = ordering[pos]
    64:         candidate_parents = ordering[:pos]
    65:         y = X[:, j]
    66:         pa_X = X[:, candidate_parents]
    67: 
    68:         gbr = GradientBoostingRegressor(
    69:             n_estimators=100, max_depth=3, learning_rate=0.1,
    70:             random_state=seed, subsample=0.8
    71:         )
    72:         gbr.fit(pa_X, y)
    73:         importances = gbr.feature_importances_
    74: 
    75:         # Keep edges with importance above threshold
    76:         threshold = 0.05
    77:         for k, p in enumerate(candidate_parents):
    78:             if importances[k] > threshold:
    79:                 B[j, p] = 1.0  # B[child, parent] = 1 means parent -> child
    80: 
    81:     # --- Step 3: Prune spurious edges via partial residual independence test ---
    82:     for j in range(n_vars):
    83:         parents = list(np.where(B[j, :] != 0)[0])
    84:         if len(parents) <= 1:
    85:             continue
    86:         to_remove = []
    87:         for p in parents:
    88:             other_parents = [pp for pp in parents if pp != p]
    89:             if len(other_parents) == 0:
    90:                 continue
    91:             # Regress j on other parents
    92:             gbr_j = GradientBoostingRegressor(
    93:                 n_estimators=50, max_depth=3, learning_rate=0.1,
    94:                 random_state=seed
    95:             )
    96:             gbr_j.fit(X[:, other_parents], X[:, j])
    97:             resid_j = X[:, j] - gbr_j.predict(X[:, other_parents])
    98:             # Regress p on other parents
    99:             gbr_p = GradientBoostingRegressor(
   100:                 n_estimators=50, max_depth=3, learning_rate=0.1,
   101:                 random_state=seed
   102:             )
   103:             gbr_p.fit(X[:, other_parents], X[:, p])
   104:             resid_p = X[:, p] - gbr_p.predict(X[:, other_parents])
   105:             # Check correlation of partial residuals
   106:             corr = np.abs(np.corrcoef(resid_j, resid_p)[0, 1])
   107:             if corr < 0.05:
   108:                 to_remove.append(p)
   109:         for p in to_remove:
   110:             B[j, p] = 0.0
   111: 
   112:     return B
   113: # =====================================================================

notears_mlp 基线 — 可编辑区域 [只读 — 参考实现]

在 causal-learn/bench/custom_algorithm.py 中：

python
Lines 3–162:
     1: import numpy as np
     2: 
     3: # =====================================================================
     4: # EDITABLE: implement run_causal_discovery below
     5: # =====================================================================
     6: def run_causal_discovery(X: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
     7:     """
     8:     Input:  X of shape (n_samples, n_variables)
     9:     Output: adjacency matrix B of shape (n_variables, n_variables)
    10:             B[i, j] != 0  means j -> i  (follows causal-learn convention)
    11:     """
    12:     import os
    13:     import numpy as np
    14:     from scipy.optimize import minimize
    15: 
    16:     n_samples, n_vars = X.shape
    17:     seed = int(os.environ.get("SEED", "42"))
    18: 
    19:     # --- Hyperparameters ---
    20:     max_iter = 30
    21:     h_tol = 1e-8
    22:     rho_max = 1e+16
    23:     w_threshold = 0.3
    24:     lambda1 = 0.01  # L1 penalty
    25: 
    26:     # Standardize data
    27:     X_std = (X - X.mean(axis=0)) / (X.std(axis=0) + 1e-8)
    28: 
    29:     # --- NOTEARS (linear) on the data ---
    30:     # Formulation: minimize 0.5/n * ||X - X W||^2_F + lambda1 * |W|_1
    31:     # subject to h(W) = tr(e^{W o W}) - d = 0
    32: 
    33:     def _h(W):
    34:         """Acyclicity constraint: h(W) = tr(e^{W o W}) - d."""
    35:         M = W * W
    36:         # Matrix exponential trace via power series (consistent with _h_grad)
    37:         expm_M = np.eye(n_vars)
    38:         power = np.eye(n_vars)
    39:         for k in range(1, 12):
    40:             power = power @ M / k
    41:             expm_M += power
    42:         return np.trace(expm_M) - n_vars
    43: 
    44:     def _h_grad(W):
    45:         """Gradient of h w.r.t. W."""
    46:         M = W * W
    47:         # expm(M) via series (10 terms)
    48:         expm_M = np.eye(n_vars)
    49:         power = np.eye(n_vars)
    50:         for k in range(1, 12):
    51:             power = power @ M / k
    52:             expm_M += power
    53:         return 2 * W * expm_M
    54: 
    55:     def _loss_and_grad(W_flat, rho, alpha):
    56:         W = W_flat.reshape(n_vars, n_vars)
    57:         # Zero diagonal (no self-loops)
    58:         np.fill_diagonal(W, 0)
    59: 
    60:         # MSE loss: 0.5/n * ||X - XW||^2
    61:         R = X_std - X_std @ W  # (n, d)
    62:         loss = 0.5 / n_samples * np.sum(R ** 2)
    63:         # Gradient of MSE w.r.t. W
    64:         G_mse = -1.0 / n_samples * (X_std.T @ R)  # (d, d)
    65: 
    66:         # L1 penalty
    67:         l1_loss = lambda1 * np.sum(np.abs(W))
    68:         G_l1 = lambda1 * np.sign(W)
    69: 
    70:         # Acyclicity
    71:         h_val = _h(W)
    72:         G_h = _h_grad(W)
    73: 
    74:         total_loss = loss + l1_loss + 0.5 * rho * h_val ** 2 + alpha * h_val
    75:         G_total = G_mse + G_l1 + (rho * h_val + alpha) * G_h
    76: 
    77:         # Zero diagonal gradient
    78:         np.fill_diagonal(G_total, 0)
    79: 
    80:         return total_loss, G_total.ravel()
    81: 
    82:     # --- Augmented Lagrangian ---
    83:     # Small random init to break symmetry (zeros can stall the optimizer)
    84:     rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)
    85:     W_est = rng.randn(n_vars, n_vars) * 0.01
    86:     np.fill_diagonal(W_est, 0)
    87:     rho = 1.0
    88:     alpha_dual = 0.0
    89:     h_prev = np.inf
    90: 
    91:     for _ in range(max_iter):
    92:         result = minimize(
    93:             lambda w: _loss_and_grad(w, rho, alpha_dual),
    94:             W_est.ravel(),
    95:             method='L-BFGS-B',
    96:             jac=True,
    97:             options={'maxiter': 500}
    98:         )
    99:         W_est = result.x.reshape(n_vars, n_vars)
   100:         np.fill_diagonal(W_est, 0)
   101: 
   102:         h_new = _h(W_est)
   103:         if h_new > 0.25 * h_prev:
   104:             rho *= 10.0
   105:         alpha_dual += rho * h_new
   106:         h_prev = h_new
   107: 
   108:         if abs(h_new) < h_tol or rho > rho_max:
   109:             break
   110: 
   111:     # --- Now do nonlinear refinement: for each variable, use kernel regression ---
   112:     # Use the linear NOTEARS skeleton and refine with nonlinear regression
   113:     from sklearn.ensemble import GradientBoostingRegressor
   114: 
   115:     # Threshold the linear result
   116:     W_abs = np.abs(W_est)
   117:     W_abs[W_abs < w_threshold] = 0.0
   118: 
   119:     # Refine: for each node, check if candidate parents improve nonlinear fit
   120:     B = np.zeros((n_vars, n_vars))
   121:     for j in range(n_vars):
   122:         # Candidate parents from linear NOTEARS
   123:         candidates = np.where(W_abs[:, j] > 0)[0].tolist()
   124:         # Also add strong linear correlations as candidates
   125:         for i in range(n_vars):
   126:             if i == j:
   127:                 continue
   128:             corr = np.abs(np.corrcoef(X_std[:, i], X_std[:, j])[0, 1])
   129:             if corr > 0.15 and i not in candidates:
   130:                 candidates.append(i)
   131:         if not candidates:
   132:             continue
   133: 
   134:         # Nonlinear regression from candidates to j
   135:         gbr = GradientBoostingRegressor(
   136:             n_estimators=100, max_depth=3, learning_rate=0.1,
   137:             random_state=seed, subsample=0.8
   138:         )
   139:         gbr.fit(X_std[:, candidates], X_std[:, j])
   140:         importances = gbr.feature_importances_
   141: 
   142:         for k, p in enumerate(candidates):
   143:             if importances[k] > 0.05:
   144:                 B[j, p] = 1.0
   145: 
   146:     # Enforce DAG by removing cycles using topological ordering from linear NOTEARS
   147:     # Use causal order from W_est
   148:     order_score = np.sum(np.abs(W_est), axis=0)  # higher = more downstream
   149:     topo_order = np.argsort(order_score)  # ascending = more root-like first
   150:     rank = np.zeros(n_vars, dtype=int)
   151:     for idx, node in enumerate(topo_order):
   152:         rank[node] = idx
   153: 
   154:     # Remove edges that violate topological ordering
   155:     for i in range(n_vars):
   156:         for j in range(n_vars):
   157:             if B[i, j] != 0 and rank[j] >= rank[i]:
   158:                 # j -> i but j is downstream of i: remove
   159:                 B[i, j] = 0.0
   160: 
   161:     return B
   162: # =====================================================================

directlingam 基线 — 可编辑区域 [只读 — 参考实现]

在 causal-learn/bench/custom_algorithm.py 中：

python
Lines 3–78:
     1: import numpy as np
     2: 
     3: # =====================================================================
     4: # EDITABLE: implement run_causal_discovery below
     5: # =====================================================================
     6: def run_causal_discovery(X: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
     7:     """
     8:     Input:  X of shape (n_samples, n_variables)
     9:     Output: adjacency matrix B of shape (n_variables, n_variables)
    10:             B[i, j] != 0  means j -> i  (follows causal-learn convention)
    11:     """
    12:     import os
    13:     import numpy as np
    14:     from sklearn.utils import check_array
    15:     from causallearn.search.FCMBased.lingam.base import _BaseLiNGAM
    16: 
    17:     X = check_array(X)
    18:     seed = int(os.environ.get("SEED", "42"))
    19:     n_features = X.shape[1]
    20: 
    21:     # Core DirectLiNGAM steps (from causallearn.search.FCMBased.lingam.direct_lingam)
    22:     def _residual(xi: np.ndarray, xj: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    23:         return xi - (np.cov(xi, xj)[0, 1] / np.var(xj)) * xj
    24: 
    25:     def _entropy(u: np.ndarray) -> float:
    26:         k1 = 79.047
    27:         k2 = 7.4129
    28:         gamma = 0.37457
    29:         return (1 + np.log(2 * np.pi)) / 2 - \
    30:                k1 * (np.mean(np.log(np.cosh(u))) - gamma) ** 2 - \
    31:                k2 * (np.mean(u * np.exp((-u ** 2) / 2))) ** 2
    32: 
    33:     def _diff_mutual_info(
    34:         xi_std: np.ndarray,
    35:         xj_std: np.ndarray,
    36:         ri_j: np.ndarray,
    37:         rj_i: np.ndarray,
    38:     ) -> float:
    39:         return (_entropy(xj_std) + _entropy(ri_j / np.std(ri_j))) - \
    40:                (_entropy(xi_std) + _entropy(rj_i / np.std(rj_i)))
    41: 
    42:     def _search_causal_order(X_work: np.ndarray, U: np.ndarray) -> int:
    43:         if len(U) == 1:
    44:             return int(U[0])
    45:         M_list = []
    46:         for i in U:
    47:             M = 0.0
    48:             for j in U:
    49:                 if i == j:
    50:                     continue
    51:                 xi_std = (X_work[:, i] - np.mean(X_work[:, i])) / np.std(X_work[:, i])
    52:                 xj_std = (X_work[:, j] - np.mean(X_work[:, j])) / np.std(X_work[:, j])
    53:                 ri_j = _residual(xi_std, xj_std)
    54:                 rj_i = _residual(xj_std, xi_std)
    55:                 M += np.min([0.0, _diff_mutual_info(xi_std, xj_std, ri_j, rj_i)]) ** 2
    56:             M_list.append(-1.0 * M)
    57:         return int(U[np.argmax(M_list)])
    58: 
    59:     U = np.arange(n_features)
    60:     K = []
    61:     X_work = np.copy(X)
    62:     for _ in range(n_features):
    63:         m = _search_causal_order(X_work, U)
    64:         for i in U:
    65:             if i != m:
    66:                 X_work[:, i] = _residual(X_work[:, i], X_work[:, m])
    67:         K.append(m)
    68:         U = U[U != m]
    69: 
    70:     class _LocalDirectLiNGAM(_BaseLiNGAM):
    71:         def fit(self, X):
    72:             return self
    73: 
    74:     model = _LocalDirectLiNGAM(random_state=seed)
    75:     model._causal_order = K
    76:     model._estimate_adjacency_matrix(X)
    77:     return model.adjacency_matrix_
    78: # =====================================================================

grandag 基线 — 可编辑区域 [只读 — 参考实现]

在 causal-learn/bench/custom_algorithm.py 中：

python
Lines 3–213:
     1: import numpy as np
     2: 
     3: # =====================================================================
     4: # EDITABLE: implement run_causal_discovery below
     5: # =====================================================================
     6: def run_causal_discovery(X: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
     7:     """GraN-DAG (Lachapelle et al., ICLR 2020).
     8: 
     9:     B[i,j] != 0 means j -> i (causal-learn convention).
    10:     """
    11:     import os
    12:     import torch, torch.nn as nn, torch.nn.functional as F
    13:     from torch import distributions
    14: 
    15:     seed = int(os.environ.get("SEED", "42"))
    16:     torch.set_num_threads(2)
    17:     torch.manual_seed(seed)
    18:     np.random.seed(seed)
    19: 
    20:     n, d = X.shape
    21:     DT = torch.float64
    22: 
    23:     # ================================================================== #
    24:     # Per-variable MLP model (NonlinearGaussANM, 2x10, leaky-relu)       #
    25:     # ================================================================== #
    26:     class _M(nn.Module):
    27:         def __init__(self):
    28:             super().__init__()
    29:             # Explicit adjacency mask (not a Parameter -- updated by clamping only)
    30:             self.adjacency = torch.ones(d, d, dtype=DT) - torch.eye(d, dtype=DT)
    31:             layers = [d, 10, 10, 1]  # [input, hidden1, hidden2, output_dim]
    32:             self.wt = nn.ParameterList()
    33:             self.bi = nn.ParameterList()
    34:             for k in range(len(layers) - 1):
    35:                 self.wt.append(nn.Parameter(
    36:                     torch.zeros(d, layers[k + 1], layers[k], dtype=DT)))
    37:                 self.bi.append(nn.Parameter(
    38:                     torch.zeros(d, layers[k + 1], dtype=DT)))
    39:             # Per-variable learnable noise log-std (ANM model)
    40:             self.log_std = nn.ParameterList(
    41:                 [nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(1, dtype=DT)) for _ in range(d)])
    42:             # Xavier init matching gcastle's reset_params() order
    43:             g = nn.init.calculate_gain('leaky_relu')
    44:             with torch.no_grad():
    45:                 for nd in range(d):
    46:                     for w in self.wt:
    47:                         nn.init.xavier_uniform_(w[nd], gain=g)
    48:                     for b in self.bi:
    49:                         b[nd].zero_()
    50: 
    51:         def _fwd(self, x):
    52:             """Per-variable forward pass with adjacency masking on first layer."""
    53:             for k in range(3):
    54:                 if k == 0:
    55:                     x = torch.einsum("tij,ljt,bj->bti", self.wt[k],
    56:                                      self.adjacency.unsqueeze(0), x) \
    57:                         + self.bi[k]
    58:                 else:
    59:                     x = torch.einsum("tij,btj->bti", self.wt[k], x) \
    60:                         + self.bi[k]
    61:                 if k < 2:
    62:                     x = F.leaky_relu(x)
    63:             return torch.unbind(x, 1)  # d tensors of (batch, 1)
    64: 
    65:         def log_lik(self, x, detach_target=False):
    66:             """(batch, d) per-variable Gaussian log-likelihoods."""
    67:             preds = self._fwd(x)
    68:             parts = []
    69:             for i in range(d):
    70:                 mu = preds[i].squeeze(1)
    71:                 sig = torch.exp(self.log_std[i])
    72:                 xi = x[:, i].detach() if detach_target else x[:, i]
    73:                 parts.append(
    74:                     distributions.Normal(mu, sig).log_prob(xi).unsqueeze(1))
    75:             return torch.cat(parts, 1)
    76: 
    77:         def w_adj(self):
    78:             """Weighted adjacency via product of |weights|, path-normalised."""
    79:             prod = torch.eye(d, dtype=DT)
    80:             pn = torch.eye(d, dtype=DT)
    81:             off = (1.0 - torch.eye(d, dtype=DT)).unsqueeze(0)
    82:             for i, w in enumerate(self.wt):
    83:                 wa = torch.abs(w)
    84:                 if i == 0:
    85:                     prod = torch.einsum("tij,ljt,jk->tik",
    86:                                         wa, self.adjacency.unsqueeze(0), prod)
    87:                     pn = torch.einsum("tij,ljt,jk->tik",
    88:                                       torch.ones_like(wa), off, pn)
    89:                 else:
    90:                     prod = torch.einsum("tij,tjk->tik", wa, prod)
    91:                     pn = torch.einsum("tij,tjk->tik",
    92:                                       torch.ones_like(wa), pn)
    93:             prod = prod.sum(1)
    94:             pn = pn.sum(1)
    95:             return (prod / (pn + torch.eye(d, dtype=DT))).t()
    96: 
    97:     mdl = _M()
    98: 
    99:     # ================================================================== #
   100:     # Data split (80/20, no shuffle, no normalise -- gcastle defaults)    #
   101:     # ================================================================== #
   102:     tn = int(n * 0.8)
   103:     Xtr = torch.as_tensor(X[:tn], dtype=DT)
   104:     Xte = torch.as_tensor(X[tn:], dtype=DT)
   105:     rng_tr = np.random.RandomState(seed)
   106:     rng_te = np.random.RandomState(seed + 1)
   107: 
   108:     def _samp(data, rng, bs):
   109:         idx = rng.choice(data.shape[0], size=int(bs), replace=False)
   110:         return data[torch.as_tensor(idx).long()]
   111: 
   112:     # ================================================================== #
   113:     # Augmented-Lagrangian training (convergence-based mu/lambda update)  #
   114:     # ================================================================== #
   115:     mu, lamb = 0.001, 0.0        # penalty & dual variable
   116:     opt = torch.optim.RMSprop(mdl.parameters(), lr=0.001)
   117:     a_val, nns, hh = [], [], []  # validation AL, not-nll, constraint history
   118:     BS, ITER, WIN = min(64, tn), 30000, 100
   119: 
   120:     for it in range(ITER):
   121:         mdl.train()
   122:         xb = _samp(Xtr, rng_tr, BS)
   123:         loss = -mdl.log_lik(xb).mean()
   124:         mdl.eval()
   125: 
   126:         wa = mdl.w_adj()
   127:         h = torch.trace(torch.matrix_exp(wa)) - d
   128:         al = loss + 0.5 * mu * h ** 2 + lamb * h
   129: 
   130:         opt.zero_grad()
   131:         al.backward()
   132:         opt.step()
   133: 
   134:         # Edge clamping -- only apply periodically to avoid premature
   135:         # irreversible edge removal that causes instability across runs.
   136:         # gcastle default threshold is 1e-4, but applying every step is
   137:         # too aggressive; applying every 500 iterations with a stricter
   138:         # weight threshold (1e-3) is more stable.
   139:         if it > 0 and it % 500 == 0:
   140:             with torch.no_grad():
   141:                 mdl.adjacency *= (wa > 1e-3).to(DT)
   142: 
   143:         nns.append(0.5 * mu * h.item() ** 2 + lamb * h.item())
   144: 
   145:         # Validation every WIN iterations
   146:         if it % WIN == 0:
   147:             with torch.no_grad():
   148:                 vl = -mdl.log_lik(
   149:                     _samp(Xte, rng_te, Xte.shape[0])).mean()
   150:                 a_val.append([it, vl.item() + nns[-1]])
   151: 
   152:         # Convergence delta (checked every 2*WIN)
   153:         dl = -np.inf
   154:         if it >= 2 * WIN and it % (2 * WIN) == 0:
   155:             t0, th, t1 = a_val[-3][1], a_val[-2][1], a_val[-1][1]
   156:             if not (min(t0, t1) < th < max(t0, t1)):
   157:                 dl = -np.inf
   158:             else:
   159:                 dl = (t1 - t0) / WIN
   160: 
   161:         # Lambda / mu update
   162:         if h.item() > 1e-8:
   163:             if abs(dl) < 1e-3 or dl > 0:
   164:                 lamb += mu * h.item()
   165:                 hh.append(h.item())
   166:                 if len(hh) >= 2 and hh[-1] > hh[-2] * 0.9:
   167:                     mu *= 10
   168:                 # Adjust moving-average validation to account for new mu/lambda
   169:                 gap = (0.5 * mu * h.item() ** 2
   170:                        + lamb * h.item() - nns[-1])
   171:                 a_val[-1][1] += gap
   172:                 opt = torch.optim.RMSprop(mdl.parameters(), lr=0.001)
   173:         else:
   174:             # Converged -- final clamping of zero-weight edges
   175:             with torch.no_grad():
   176:                 mdl.adjacency *= (wa > 0).to(DT)
   177:             break
   178: 
   179:     # ================================================================== #
   180:     # DAG enforcement: Jacobian threshold + weakest-edge removal          #
   181:     # ================================================================== #
   182:     mdl.eval()
   183:     xj = Xtr.clone().requires_grad_(True)
   184:     ll = mdl.log_lik(xj, detach_target=True)       # (tn, d)
   185:     lps = torch.unbind(ll, 1)                       # d tensors of (tn,)
   186:     jac = torch.zeros(d, d, dtype=DT)
   187:     for i in range(d):
   188:         g = torch.autograd.grad(
   189:             lps[i], xj, retain_graph=True,
   190:             grad_outputs=torch.ones(Xtr.shape[0], dtype=DT))[0]
   191:         jac[i] = g.abs().mean(0)
   192:     A = jac.t().detach().numpy()
   193: 
   194:     # Find smallest threshold that produces an acyclic graph
   195:     with torch.no_grad():
   196:         for thr in np.unique(A):
   197:             keep = torch.tensor(A > thr + 1e-8, dtype=DT)
   198:             na = mdl.adjacency * keep
   199:             # Acyclicity check via matrix-power trace
   200:             prod = torch.eye(d, dtype=DT)
   201:             ok = True
   202:             for _ in range(d):
   203:                 prod = na @ prod
   204:                 if prod.trace() != 0:
   205:                     ok = False
   206:                     break
   207:             if ok:
   208:                 mdl.adjacency = na
   209:                 break
   210: 
   211:     # adj[j,t]=1 means j->t; B[i,j]=1 means j->i  =>  B = adj.T
   212:     return mdl.adjacency.t().detach().numpy()
   213: # =====================================================================

🇬🇧 [English Original] Causal Observational (Nonlinear)

MLS-Bench: causal-observational-nonlinear

Causal Discovery: Observational Nonlinear Data (Additive Noise Models)

Research Question

Implement a causal discovery algorithm that recovers the directed DAG from purely observational data generated by a nonlinear Additive Noise Model (ANM).

Background

Additive Noise Models assume structural equations of the form x_j = f_j(parents(j)) + e_j, where f_j are nonlinear functions and e_j are independent noise terms. Under mild conditions on f_j and the noise distribution, the causal DAG is identifiable from observational data alone (Hoyer, Janzing, Mooij, Peters & Scholkopf, "Nonlinear causal discovery with additive noise models," NeurIPS 2008; Peters, Mooij, Janzing & Scholkopf, "Causal Discovery with Continuous Additive Noise Models," JMLR 15, 2014). This goes beyond linear methods (LiNGAM, PC, GES) that assume linearity.

Key challenges include: (1) testing independence in high-dimensional nonlinear
settings, (2) scaling to larger graphs, (3) handling diverse nonlinear function
types (MLP, GP, polynomial, sigmoid), and (4) robustness when noise is
Gaussian, where ANM identifiability becomes more delicate.

Task

Implement run_causal_discovery(X) in causal-learn/bench/custom_algorithm.py. It must return a directed DAG compatible with the benchmark evaluation.

Reference baselines

cam: Causal Additive Models with GAM regression. Score-based. Buhlmann,
Peters & Ernest, "CAM: Causal additive models, high-dimensional order search and penalized regression," Annals of Statistics 42(6), 2014 (arXiv:1310.1533).
notears_mlp: NOTEARS with MLP parametrization for nonlinear additive
structure. Continuous optimization. Zheng, Dan, Aragam, Ravikumar & Xing, "Learning Sparse Nonparametric DAGs," AISTATS 2020 (arXiv:1909.13189). The default uses a one-hidden-layer MLP with sigmoid activation as in the paper.
directlingam: Linear reference baseline. Shimizu et al., JMLR 12, 2011
(arXiv:1101.2489).
grandag: GraN-DAG. Lachapelle, Brouillard, Deleu & Lacoste-Julien,
"Gradient-Based Neural DAG Learning," ICLR 2020 (arXiv:1906.02226). Gradient-based DAG learning using neural conditional densities with an augmented-Lagrangian acyclicity constraint.

Suitable contributions include nonlinear independence tests, additive-noise
scoring, differentiable DAG learning, hybrid ordering methods, or other
modular procedures that exploit nonlinear causal asymmetry while remaining
practical at the evaluated graph sizes.

/workspace/causal-learn/ is the research scaffold for this task.
