cam baseline — editable region [READ-ONLY — reference implementation]

In causal-learn/bench/custom_algorithm.py:

python
Lines 3–113:
     1: import numpy as np
     2: 
     3: # =====================================================================
     4: # EDITABLE: implement run_causal_discovery below
     5: # =====================================================================
     6: def run_causal_discovery(X: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
     7:     """
     8:     Input:  X of shape (n_samples, n_variables)
     9:     Output: adjacency matrix B of shape (n_variables, n_variables)
    10:             B[i, j] != 0  means j -> i  (follows causal-learn convention)
    11:     """
    12:     import os
    13:     import numpy as np
    14:     from sklearn.ensemble import GradientBoostingRegressor
    15:     from sklearn.model_selection import cross_val_score
    16: 
    17:     n_samples, n_vars = X.shape
    18:     seed = int(os.environ.get("SEED", "42"))
    19: 
    20:     # --- Step 1: Estimate causal ordering via greedy score-based search ---
    21:     # For each variable, compute residual variance after nonlinear regression
    22:     # on candidate parents. Variables with lower residual variance given
    23:     # earlier variables are placed later in the ordering.
    24: 
    25:     remaining = list(range(n_vars))
    26:     ordering = []
    27: 
    28:     while remaining:
    29:         if len(ordering) == 0:
    30:             # First variable: pick the one with highest marginal variance
    31:             # (root causes tend to have variance = noise variance only)
    32:             scores = []
    33:             for j in remaining:
    34:                 scores.append(np.var(X[:, j]))
    35:             # Pick the one with smallest variance (likely a root)
    36:             best_idx = np.argmin(scores)
    37:             ordering.append(remaining.pop(best_idx))
    38:         else:
    39:             # For each remaining var, fit nonlinear regression on current ordering
    40:             best_score = np.inf
    41:             best_var = None
    42:             best_var_idx = None
    43:             parents_X = X[:, ordering]
    44:             for idx, j in enumerate(remaining):
    45:                 y = X[:, j]
    46:                 # Use gradient boosting as nonlinear regressor
    47:                 gbr = GradientBoostingRegressor(
    48:                     n_estimators=50, max_depth=3, learning_rate=0.1,
    49:                     random_state=seed, subsample=0.8
    50:                 )
    51:                 gbr.fit(parents_X, y)
    52:                 residuals = y - gbr.predict(parents_X)
    53:                 resid_var = np.var(residuals)
    54:                 if resid_var < best_score:
    55:                     best_score = resid_var
    56:                     best_var = j
    57:                     best_var_idx = idx
    58:             ordering.append(remaining.pop(best_var_idx))
    59: 
    60:     # --- Step 2: Preliminary adjacency via nonlinear regression along ordering ---
    61:     B = np.zeros((n_vars, n_vars))
    62:     for pos in range(1, len(ordering)):
    63:         j = ordering[pos]
    64:         candidate_parents = ordering[:pos]
    65:         y = X[:, j]
    66:         pa_X = X[:, candidate_parents]
    67: 
    68:         gbr = GradientBoostingRegressor(
    69:             n_estimators=100, max_depth=3, learning_rate=0.1,
    70:             random_state=seed, subsample=0.8
    71:         )
    72:         gbr.fit(pa_X, y)
    73:         importances = gbr.feature_importances_
    74: 
    75:         # Keep edges with importance above threshold
    76:         threshold = 0.05
    77:         for k, p in enumerate(candidate_parents):
    78:             if importances[k] > threshold:
    79:                 B[j, p] = 1.0  # B[child, parent] = 1 means parent -> child
    80: 
    81:     # --- Step 3: Prune spurious edges via partial residual independence test ---
    82:     for j in range(n_vars):
    83:         parents = list(np.where(B[j, :] != 0)[0])
    84:         if len(parents) <= 1:
    85:             continue
    86:         to_remove = []
    87:         for p in parents:
    88:             other_parents = [pp for pp in parents if pp != p]
    89:             if len(other_parents) == 0:
    90:                 continue
    91:             # Regress j on other parents
    92:             gbr_j = GradientBoostingRegressor(
    93:                 n_estimators=50, max_depth=3, learning_rate=0.1,
    94:                 random_state=seed
    95:             )
    96:             gbr_j.fit(X[:, other_parents], X[:, j])
    97:             resid_j = X[:, j] - gbr_j.predict(X[:, other_parents])
    98:             # Regress p on other parents
    99:             gbr_p = GradientBoostingRegressor(
   100:                 n_estimators=50, max_depth=3, learning_rate=0.1,
   101:                 random_state=seed
   102:             )
   103:             gbr_p.fit(X[:, other_parents], X[:, p])
   104:             resid_p = X[:, p] - gbr_p.predict(X[:, other_parents])
   105:             # Check correlation of partial residuals
   106:             corr = np.abs(np.corrcoef(resid_j, resid_p)[0, 1])
   107:             if corr < 0.05:
   108:                 to_remove.append(p)
   109:         for p in to_remove:
   110:             B[j, p] = 0.0
   111: 
   112:     return B
   113: # =====================================================================

notears_mlp baseline — editable region [READ-ONLY — reference implementation]

In causal-learn/bench/custom_algorithm.py:

python
Lines 3–162:
     1: import numpy as np
     2: 
     3: # =====================================================================
     4: # EDITABLE: implement run_causal_discovery below
     5: # =====================================================================
     6: def run_causal_discovery(X: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
     7:     """
     8:     Input:  X of shape (n_samples, n_variables)
     9:     Output: adjacency matrix B of shape (n_variables, n_variables)
    10:             B[i, j] != 0  means j -> i  (follows causal-learn convention)
    11:     """
    12:     import os
    13:     import numpy as np
    14:     from scipy.optimize import minimize
    15: 
    16:     n_samples, n_vars = X.shape
    17:     seed = int(os.environ.get("SEED", "42"))
    18: 
    19:     # --- Hyperparameters ---
    20:     max_iter = 30
    21:     h_tol = 1e-8
    22:     rho_max = 1e+16
    23:     w_threshold = 0.3
    24:     lambda1 = 0.01  # L1 penalty
    25: 
    26:     # Standardize data
    27:     X_std = (X - X.mean(axis=0)) / (X.std(axis=0) + 1e-8)
    28: 
    29:     # --- NOTEARS (linear) on the data ---
    30:     # Formulation: minimize 0.5/n * ||X - X W||^2_F + lambda1 * |W|_1
    31:     # subject to h(W) = tr(e^{W o W}) - d = 0
    32: 
    33:     def _h(W):
    34:         """Acyclicity constraint: h(W) = tr(e^{W o W}) - d."""
    35:         M = W * W
    36:         # Matrix exponential trace via power series (consistent with _h_grad)
    37:         expm_M = np.eye(n_vars)
    38:         power = np.eye(n_vars)
    39:         for k in range(1, 12):
    40:             power = power @ M / k
    41:             expm_M += power
    42:         return np.trace(expm_M) - n_vars
    43: 
    44:     def _h_grad(W):
    45:         """Gradient of h w.r.t. W."""
    46:         M = W * W
    47:         # expm(M) via series (10 terms)
    48:         expm_M = np.eye(n_vars)
    49:         power = np.eye(n_vars)
    50:         for k in range(1, 12):
    51:             power = power @ M / k
    52:             expm_M += power
    53:         return 2 * W * expm_M
    54: 
    55:     def _loss_and_grad(W_flat, rho, alpha):
    56:         W = W_flat.reshape(n_vars, n_vars)
    57:         # Zero diagonal (no self-loops)
    58:         np.fill_diagonal(W, 0)
    59: 
    60:         # MSE loss: 0.5/n * ||X - XW||^2
    61:         R = X_std - X_std @ W  # (n, d)
    62:         loss = 0.5 / n_samples * np.sum(R ** 2)
    63:         # Gradient of MSE w.r.t. W
    64:         G_mse = -1.0 / n_samples * (X_std.T @ R)  # (d, d)
    65: 
    66:         # L1 penalty
    67:         l1_loss = lambda1 * np.sum(np.abs(W))
    68:         G_l1 = lambda1 * np.sign(W)
    69: 
    70:         # Acyclicity
    71:         h_val = _h(W)
    72:         G_h = _h_grad(W)
    73: 
    74:         total_loss = loss + l1_loss + 0.5 * rho * h_val ** 2 + alpha * h_val
    75:         G_total = G_mse + G_l1 + (rho * h_val + alpha) * G_h
    76: 
    77:         # Zero diagonal gradient
    78:         np.fill_diagonal(G_total, 0)
    79: 
    80:         return total_loss, G_total.ravel()
    81: 
    82:     # --- Augmented Lagrangian ---
    83:     # Small random init to break symmetry (zeros can stall the optimizer)
    84:     rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)
    85:     W_est = rng.randn(n_vars, n_vars) * 0.01
    86:     np.fill_diagonal(W_est, 0)
    87:     rho = 1.0
    88:     alpha_dual = 0.0
    89:     h_prev = np.inf
    90: 
    91:     for _ in range(max_iter):
    92:         result = minimize(
    93:             lambda w: _loss_and_grad(w, rho, alpha_dual),
    94:             W_est.ravel(),
    95:             method='L-BFGS-B',
    96:             jac=True,
    97:             options={'maxiter': 500}
    98:         )
    99:         W_est = result.x.reshape(n_vars, n_vars)
   100:         np.fill_diagonal(W_est, 0)
   101: 
   102:         h_new = _h(W_est)
   103:         if h_new > 0.25 * h_prev:
   104:             rho *= 10.0
   105:         alpha_dual += rho * h_new
   106:         h_prev = h_new
   107: 
   108:         if abs(h_new) < h_tol or rho > rho_max:
   109:             break
   110: 
   111:     # --- Now do nonlinear refinement: for each variable, use kernel regression ---
   112:     # Use the linear NOTEARS skeleton and refine with nonlinear regression
   113:     from sklearn.ensemble import GradientBoostingRegressor
   114: 
   115:     # Threshold the linear result
   116:     W_abs = np.abs(W_est)
   117:     W_abs[W_abs < w_threshold] = 0.0
   118: 
   119:     # Refine: for each node, check if candidate parents improve nonlinear fit
   120:     B = np.zeros((n_vars, n_vars))
   121:     for j in range(n_vars):
   122:         # Candidate parents from linear NOTEARS
   123:         candidates = np.where(W_abs[:, j] > 0)[0].tolist()
   124:         # Also add strong linear correlations as candidates
   125:         for i in range(n_vars):
   126:             if i == j:
   127:                 continue
   128:             corr = np.abs(np.corrcoef(X_std[:, i], X_std[:, j])[0, 1])
   129:             if corr > 0.15 and i not in candidates:
   130:                 candidates.append(i)
   131:         if not candidates:
   132:             continue
   133: 
   134:         # Nonlinear regression from candidates to j
   135:         gbr = GradientBoostingRegressor(
   136:             n_estimators=100, max_depth=3, learning_rate=0.1,
   137:             random_state=seed, subsample=0.8
   138:         )
   139:         gbr.fit(X_std[:, candidates], X_std[:, j])
   140:         importances = gbr.feature_importances_
   141: 
   142:         for k, p in enumerate(candidates):
   143:             if importances[k] > 0.05:
   144:                 B[j, p] = 1.0
   145: 
   146:     # Enforce DAG by removing cycles using topological ordering from linear NOTEARS
   147:     # Use causal order from W_est
   148:     order_score = np.sum(np.abs(W_est), axis=0)  # higher = more downstream
   149:     topo_order = np.argsort(order_score)  # ascending = more root-like first
   150:     rank = np.zeros(n_vars, dtype=int)
   151:     for idx, node in enumerate(topo_order):
   152:         rank[node] = idx
   153: 
   154:     # Remove edges that violate topological ordering
   155:     for i in range(n_vars):
   156:         for j in range(n_vars):
   157:             if B[i, j] != 0 and rank[j] >= rank[i]:
   158:                 # j -> i but j is downstream of i: remove
   159:                 B[i, j] = 0.0
   160: 
   161:     return B
   162: # =====================================================================

directlingam baseline — editable region [READ-ONLY — reference implementation]

In causal-learn/bench/custom_algorithm.py:

python
Lines 3–78:
     1: import numpy as np
     2: 
     3: # =====================================================================
     4: # EDITABLE: implement run_causal_discovery below
     5: # =====================================================================
     6: def run_causal_discovery(X: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
     7:     """
     8:     Input:  X of shape (n_samples, n_variables)
     9:     Output: adjacency matrix B of shape (n_variables, n_variables)
    10:             B[i, j] != 0  means j -> i  (follows causal-learn convention)
    11:     """
    12:     import os
    13:     import numpy as np
    14:     from sklearn.utils import check_array
    15:     from causallearn.search.FCMBased.lingam.base import _BaseLiNGAM
    16: 
    17:     X = check_array(X)
    18:     seed = int(os.environ.get("SEED", "42"))
    19:     n_features = X.shape[1]
    20: 
    21:     # Core DirectLiNGAM steps (from causallearn.search.FCMBased.lingam.direct_lingam)
    22:     def _residual(xi: np.ndarray, xj: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    23:         return xi - (np.cov(xi, xj)[0, 1] / np.var(xj)) * xj
    24: 
    25:     def _entropy(u: np.ndarray) -> float:
    26:         k1 = 79.047
    27:         k2 = 7.4129
    28:         gamma = 0.37457
    29:         return (1 + np.log(2 * np.pi)) / 2 - \
    30:                k1 * (np.mean(np.log(np.cosh(u))) - gamma) ** 2 - \
    31:                k2 * (np.mean(u * np.exp((-u ** 2) / 2))) ** 2
    32: 
    33:     def _diff_mutual_info(
    34:         xi_std: np.ndarray,
    35:         xj_std: np.ndarray,
    36:         ri_j: np.ndarray,
    37:         rj_i: np.ndarray,
    38:     ) -> float:
    39:         return (_entropy(xj_std) + _entropy(ri_j / np.std(ri_j))) - \
    40:                (_entropy(xi_std) + _entropy(rj_i / np.std(rj_i)))
    41: 
    42:     def _search_causal_order(X_work: np.ndarray, U: np.ndarray) -> int:
    43:         if len(U) == 1:
    44:             return int(U[0])
    45:         M_list = []
    46:         for i in U:
    47:             M = 0.0
    48:             for j in U:
    49:                 if i == j:
    50:                     continue
    51:                 xi_std = (X_work[:, i] - np.mean(X_work[:, i])) / np.std(X_work[:, i])
    52:                 xj_std = (X_work[:, j] - np.mean(X_work[:, j])) / np.std(X_work[:, j])
    53:                 ri_j = _residual(xi_std, xj_std)
    54:                 rj_i = _residual(xj_std, xi_std)
    55:                 M += np.min([0.0, _diff_mutual_info(xi_std, xj_std, ri_j, rj_i)]) ** 2
    56:             M_list.append(-1.0 * M)
    57:         return int(U[np.argmax(M_list)])
    58: 
    59:     U = np.arange(n_features)
    60:     K = []
    61:     X_work = np.copy(X)
    62:     for _ in range(n_features):
    63:         m = _search_causal_order(X_work, U)
    64:         for i in U:
    65:             if i != m:
    66:                 X_work[:, i] = _residual(X_work[:, i], X_work[:, m])
    67:         K.append(m)
    68:         U = U[U != m]
    69: 
    70:     class _LocalDirectLiNGAM(_BaseLiNGAM):
    71:         def fit(self, X):
    72:             return self
    73: 
    74:     model = _LocalDirectLiNGAM(random_state=seed)
    75:     model._causal_order = K
    76:     model._estimate_adjacency_matrix(X)
    77:     return model.adjacency_matrix_
    78: # =====================================================================

grandag baseline — editable region [READ-ONLY — reference implementation]

In causal-learn/bench/custom_algorithm.py:

python
Lines 3–213:
     1: import numpy as np
     2: 
     3: # =====================================================================
     4: # EDITABLE: implement run_causal_discovery below
     5: # =====================================================================
     6: def run_causal_discovery(X: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
     7:     """GraN-DAG (Lachapelle et al., ICLR 2020).
     8: 
     9:     B[i,j] != 0 means j -> i (causal-learn convention).
    10:     """
    11:     import os
    12:     import torch, torch.nn as nn, torch.nn.functional as F
    13:     from torch import distributions
    14: 
    15:     seed = int(os.environ.get("SEED", "42"))
    16:     torch.set_num_threads(2)
    17:     torch.manual_seed(seed)
    18:     np.random.seed(seed)
    19: 
    20:     n, d = X.shape
    21:     DT = torch.float64
    22: 
    23:     # ================================================================== #
    24:     # Per-variable MLP model (NonlinearGaussANM, 2x10, leaky-relu)       #
    25:     # ================================================================== #
    26:     class _M(nn.Module):
    27:         def __init__(self):
    28:             super().__init__()
    29:             # Explicit adjacency mask (not a Parameter -- updated by clamping only)
    30:             self.adjacency = torch.ones(d, d, dtype=DT) - torch.eye(d, dtype=DT)
    31:             layers = [d, 10, 10, 1]  # [input, hidden1, hidden2, output_dim]
    32:             self.wt = nn.ParameterList()
    33:             self.bi = nn.ParameterList()
    34:             for k in range(len(layers) - 1):
    35:                 self.wt.append(nn.Parameter(
    36:                     torch.zeros(d, layers[k + 1], layers[k], dtype=DT)))
    37:                 self.bi.append(nn.Parameter(
    38:                     torch.zeros(d, layers[k + 1], dtype=DT)))
    39:             # Per-variable learnable noise log-std (ANM model)
    40:             self.log_std = nn.ParameterList(
    41:                 [nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(1, dtype=DT)) for _ in range(d)])
    42:             # Xavier init matching gcastle's reset_params() order
    43:             g = nn.init.calculate_gain('leaky_relu')
    44:             with torch.no_grad():
    45:                 for nd in range(d):
    46:                     for w in self.wt:
    47:                         nn.init.xavier_uniform_(w[nd], gain=g)
    48:                     for b in self.bi:
    49:                         b[nd].zero_()
    50: 
    51:         def _fwd(self, x):
    52:             """Per-variable forward pass with adjacency masking on first layer."""
    53:             for k in range(3):
    54:                 if k == 0:
    55:                     x = torch.einsum("tij,ljt,bj->bti", self.wt[k],
    56:                                      self.adjacency.unsqueeze(0), x) \
    57:                         + self.bi[k]
    58:                 else:
    59:                     x = torch.einsum("tij,btj->bti", self.wt[k], x) \
    60:                         + self.bi[k]
    61:                 if k < 2:
    62:                     x = F.leaky_relu(x)
    63:             return torch.unbind(x, 1)  # d tensors of (batch, 1)
    64: 
    65:         def log_lik(self, x, detach_target=False):
    66:             """(batch, d) per-variable Gaussian log-likelihoods."""
    67:             preds = self._fwd(x)
    68:             parts = []
    69:             for i in range(d):
    70:                 mu = preds[i].squeeze(1)
    71:                 sig = torch.exp(self.log_std[i])
    72:                 xi = x[:, i].detach() if detach_target else x[:, i]
    73:                 parts.append(
    74:                     distributions.Normal(mu, sig).log_prob(xi).unsqueeze(1))
    75:             return torch.cat(parts, 1)
    76: 
    77:         def w_adj(self):
    78:             """Weighted adjacency via product of |weights|, path-normalised."""
    79:             prod = torch.eye(d, dtype=DT)
    80:             pn = torch.eye(d, dtype=DT)
    81:             off = (1.0 - torch.eye(d, dtype=DT)).unsqueeze(0)
    82:             for i, w in enumerate(self.wt):
    83:                 wa = torch.abs(w)
    84:                 if i == 0:
    85:                     prod = torch.einsum("tij,ljt,jk->tik",
    86:                                         wa, self.adjacency.unsqueeze(0), prod)
    87:                     pn = torch.einsum("tij,ljt,jk->tik",
    88:                                       torch.ones_like(wa), off, pn)
    89:                 else:
    90:                     prod = torch.einsum("tij,tjk->tik", wa, prod)
    91:                     pn = torch.einsum("tij,tjk->tik",
    92:                                       torch.ones_like(wa), pn)
    93:             prod = prod.sum(1)
    94:             pn = pn.sum(1)
    95:             return (prod / (pn + torch.eye(d, dtype=DT))).t()
    96: 
    97:     mdl = _M()
    98: 
    99:     # ================================================================== #
   100:     # Data split (80/20, no shuffle, no normalise -- gcastle defaults)    #
   101:     # ================================================================== #
   102:     tn = int(n * 0.8)
   103:     Xtr = torch.as_tensor(X[:tn], dtype=DT)
   104:     Xte = torch.as_tensor(X[tn:], dtype=DT)
   105:     rng_tr = np.random.RandomState(seed)
   106:     rng_te = np.random.RandomState(seed + 1)
   107: 
   108:     def _samp(data, rng, bs):
   109:         idx = rng.choice(data.shape[0], size=int(bs), replace=False)
   110:         return data[torch.as_tensor(idx).long()]
   111: 
   112:     # ================================================================== #
   113:     # Augmented-Lagrangian training (convergence-based mu/lambda update)  #
   114:     # ================================================================== #
   115:     mu, lamb = 0.001, 0.0        # penalty & dual variable
   116:     opt = torch.optim.RMSprop(mdl.parameters(), lr=0.001)
   117:     a_val, nns, hh = [], [], []  # validation AL, not-nll, constraint history
   118:     BS, ITER, WIN = min(64, tn), 30000, 100
   119: 
   120:     for it in range(ITER):
   121:         mdl.train()
   122:         xb = _samp(Xtr, rng_tr, BS)
   123:         loss = -mdl.log_lik(xb).mean()
   124:         mdl.eval()
   125: 
   126:         wa = mdl.w_adj()
   127:         h = torch.trace(torch.matrix_exp(wa)) - d
   128:         al = loss + 0.5 * mu * h ** 2 + lamb * h
   129: 
   130:         opt.zero_grad()
   131:         al.backward()
   132:         opt.step()
   133: 
   134:         # Edge clamping -- only apply periodically to avoid premature
   135:         # irreversible edge removal that causes instability across runs.
   136:         # gcastle default threshold is 1e-4, but applying every step is
   137:         # too aggressive; applying every 500 iterations with a stricter
   138:         # weight threshold (1e-3) is more stable.
   139:         if it > 0 and it % 500 == 0:
   140:             with torch.no_grad():
   141:                 mdl.adjacency *= (wa > 1e-3).to(DT)
   142: 
   143:         nns.append(0.5 * mu * h.item() ** 2 + lamb * h.item())
   144: 
   145:         # Validation every WIN iterations
   146:         if it % WIN == 0:
   147:             with torch.no_grad():
   148:                 vl = -mdl.log_lik(
   149:                     _samp(Xte, rng_te, Xte.shape[0])).mean()
   150:                 a_val.append([it, vl.item() + nns[-1]])
   151: 
   152:         # Convergence delta (checked every 2*WIN)
   153:         dl = -np.inf
   154:         if it >= 2 * WIN and it % (2 * WIN) == 0:
   155:             t0, th, t1 = a_val[-3][1], a_val[-2][1], a_val[-1][1]
   156:             if not (min(t0, t1) < th < max(t0, t1)):
   157:                 dl = -np.inf
   158:             else:
   159:                 dl = (t1 - t0) / WIN
   160: 
   161:         # Lambda / mu update
   162:         if h.item() > 1e-8:
   163:             if abs(dl) < 1e-3 or dl > 0:
   164:                 lamb += mu * h.item()
   165:                 hh.append(h.item())
   166:                 if len(hh) >= 2 and hh[-1] > hh[-2] * 0.9:
   167:                     mu *= 10
   168:                 # Adjust moving-average validation to account for new mu/lambda
   169:                 gap = (0.5 * mu * h.item() ** 2
   170:                        + lamb * h.item() - nns[-1])
   171:                 a_val[-1][1] += gap
   172:                 opt = torch.optim.RMSprop(mdl.parameters(), lr=0.001)
   173:         else:
   174:             # Converged -- final clamping of zero-weight edges
   175:             with torch.no_grad():
   176:                 mdl.adjacency *= (wa > 0).to(DT)
   177:             break
   178: 
   179:     # ================================================================== #
   180:     # DAG enforcement: Jacobian threshold + weakest-edge removal          #
   181:     # ================================================================== #
   182:     mdl.eval()
   183:     xj = Xtr.clone().requires_grad_(True)
   184:     ll = mdl.log_lik(xj, detach_target=True)       # (tn, d)
   185:     lps = torch.unbind(ll, 1)                       # d tensors of (tn,)
   186:     jac = torch.zeros(d, d, dtype=DT)
   187:     for i in range(d):
   188:         g = torch.autograd.grad(
   189:             lps[i], xj, retain_graph=True,
   190:             grad_outputs=torch.ones(Xtr.shape[0], dtype=DT))[0]
   191:         jac[i] = g.abs().mean(0)
   192:     A = jac.t().detach().numpy()
   193: 
   194:     # Find smallest threshold that produces an acyclic graph
   195:     with torch.no_grad():
   196:         for thr in np.unique(A):
   197:             keep = torch.tensor(A > thr + 1e-8, dtype=DT)
   198:             na = mdl.adjacency * keep
   199:             # Acyclicity check via matrix-power trace
   200:             prod = torch.eye(d, dtype=DT)
   201:             ok = True
   202:             for _ in range(d):
   203:                 prod = na @ prod
   204:                 if prod.trace() != 0:
   205:                     ok = False
   206:                     break
   207:             if ok:
   208:                 mdl.adjacency = na
   209:                 break
   210: 
   211:     # adj[j,t]=1 means j->t; B[i,j]=1 means j->i  =>  B = adj.T
   212:     return mdl.adjacency.t().detach().numpy()
   213: # =====================================================================

📄 §5.5 causal-treatment-effect — 模型看到的主文档：中文翻译 + 英文原文（均默认折叠）
🇨🇳 [中文翻译] Causal Treatment Effect

MLS-Bench: causal-treatment-effect

因果处理效应估计

研究问题

设计一个新颖的估计器，用于从观测数据中估计条件平均处理效应（CATE），要求其准确、对混杂具有鲁棒性，并能在多种合成数据生成过程之间泛化。

背景

估计异质性处理效应——即处理的因果效应如何在个体之间变化——是因果推断中的一个核心问题。给定包含协变量 X、二值处理 T 和结果 Y 的观测数据，目标是估计 tau(x) = E[Y(1) - Y(0) | X = x]，即条件平均处理效应。

主要挑战包括：

混杂：处理分配依赖于协变量，因此朴素的比较是有偏的。

异质性：处理效应在整个协变量空间中以复杂、非线性的方式变化。

模型误设：真实的响应面可能不符合参数化假设。

双重稳健性：理想情况下，只要结果模型或倾向得分模型之一正确，估计器就是一致的。

经典方法包括 S-Learner（单模型）、T-Learner（为每个处理组分别拟合结果模型）和 IPW（倾向得分重加权）。现代方法利用正交化或去偏技术以获得更好的收敛速率：参见 Athey & Wager, "Estimation and Inference of Heterogeneous Treatment Effects using Random Forests," JASA 113(523), 2018 (arXiv:1510.04342)；Kennedy, "Towards optimal doubly robust estimation of heterogeneous causal effects," Electronic Journal of Statistics 17(2), 2023 (arXiv:2004.14497)；以及 Nie & Wager, "Quasi-Oracle Estimation of Heterogeneous Treatment Effects," Biometrika 108(2), 2021 (arXiv:1712.04912)。

任务

修改 custom_cate.py 中的 CATEEstimator 类。该估计器必须实现： 

python
class CATEEstimator:
    def fit(self, X, T, Y) -> "CATEEstimator":
        """Learn from observational covariates X, binary treatment T, outcome Y."""

    def predict(self, X):
        """Return predicted individual treatment effects tau_hat for each row of X."""

scikit-learn、numpy 和 scipy 均可用。

有效的方案可以组合结果建模、倾向得分建模、正交化、加权、残差化、森林方法、神经网络模型或其他模块化的 CATE 思路，只要能够应对混杂和处理效应异质性即可。

你的工作区

你正在 /workspace 中工作。软件包源码树 /workspace/scikit-learn/ 是本任务的研究脚手架。

这些行号标示的是一个可编辑的区域，而不是固定的行数预算：你可以在该区域内增删行。只有可编辑范围之外的代码必须保持不变。

    84: class CATEEstimator(BaseCATEEstimator):
    85:     """Custom CATE (Conditional Average Treatment Effect) estimator.
    86:

    88:     observational data. Your estimator receives covariates X, binary
    89:     treatment T, and outcomes Y, and must estimate tau(x) = E[Y(1)-Y(0)|X=x].
    90: 
    91:     Key challenges:
    92:     - Confounding: treatment assignment depends on covariates
    93:     - Heterogeneity: treatment effects vary across individuals
    94:     - Model misspecification: response surfaces may be nonlinear
    95:     - Finite-sample performance: must work well with limited data
    96: 
    97:     Approaches to consider:
    98:     - Meta-learners (S/T/X/R/DR-Learner frameworks)
    99:     - Propensity score methods (weighting, matching, doubly robust)
   100:     - Tree-based methods (causal forests, Bayesian additive regression trees)
   101:     - Representation learning for treatment effects
   102:     - Kernel methods or local regression for CATE
   103:     - Ensemble methods combining multiple estimators
   104: 
   105:     Available imports (in FIXED section above):
   106:         numpy, scipy.stats, sklearn (all submodules)
   107: 
   108:     Interface contract:
   109:         fit(X, T, Y) -> self
   110:         predict(X) -> tau_hat of shape (n,)
   111:     """

   115:

   121:         """Fit the estimator on observational data.
   122:

   134:         # Placeholder: simple S-Learner (augmented features)
   135:         n, p = X.shape
   136:         XT = np.column_stack([X, T.reshape(-1, 1)])
   137:         self._model = Ridge(alpha=1.0)
   138:         self._model.fit(XT, Y)
   139:         return self

   143:

   149:

   152:         n = X.shape[0]
   153:         X1 = np.column_stack([X, np.ones((n, 1))])
   154:         X0 = np.column_stack([X, np.zeros((n, 1))])
   155:         return self._model.predict(X1) - self._model.predict(X0)
   156:

   172: 
   173: 
   174: def load_inputs(dataset, data_seed):
   175:     """Load the pre-generated observational inputs for (dataset, data_seed).
   176: 
   177:     Only (X, T, Y) are available — the true treatment effect is held out by the
   178:     harness and is not present in this process.
   179:     """
   180:     path = os.path.join(_inputs_dir(), f"{dataset}_seed{data_seed}.npz.b64")
   181:     with open(path, "r") as f:
   182:         raw = base64.b64decode(f.read())
   183:     with np.load(io.BytesIO(raw)) as d:
   184:         return d["X"], d["T"], d["Y"]
   185: 
   186: 
   187: def cross_fit_predict(X, T, Y, n_splits, seed):
   188:     """Cross-fitted CATE prediction (same K-fold protocol as before).
   189: 
   190:     Fit on K-1 folds, predict held-out fold; aggregate to per-row tau_hat.
   191:     A fresh deep-copy of the estimator is used per fold (cross-fitting).
   192:     """
   193:     import copy
   194:     base = CATEEstimator()
   195:     kf = KFold(n_splits=n_splits, shuffle=True, random_state=seed)
   196:     tau_hat_all = np.zeros(len(X))
   197:     for train_idx, test_idx in kf.split(X):
   198:         est = copy.deepcopy(base)
   199:         est.fit(X[train_idx], T[train_idx], Y[train_idx])
   200:         tau_hat_all[test_idx] = est.predict(X[test_idx])
   201:     return tau_hat_all
   202: 
   203: 
   204: def main():
   205:     parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="CATE Estimation Benchmark")
   206:     parser.add_argument("--dataset", type=str, required=True,
   207:                         help="Opaque dataset token (the real dataset is held out)")
   208:     parser.add_argument("--seed", type=int, default=42,
   209:                         help="Base random seed (selects the pre-generated inputs)")
   210:     parser.add_argument("--n-splits", type=int, default=5,
   211:                         help="Number of cross-validation folds")
   212:     parser.add_argument("--n-reps", type=int, default=10,
   213:                         help="Number of repetitions with different data seeds")
   214:     args = parser.parse_args()
   215: 
   216:     print(f"Evaluating on {args.dataset} (seed={args.seed}, "
   217:           f"n_splits={args.n_splits}, n_reps={args.n_reps})", flush=True)
   218: 
   219:     start = time.time()
   220:     for rep in range(args.n_reps):
   221:         data_seed = args.seed + rep * 1000
   222:         X, T, Y = load_inputs(args.dataset, data_seed)
   223:         tau_hat = cross_fit_predict(X, T, Y, args.n_splits, data_seed)
   224: 
   225:         # Emit the cross-fitted predictions for the held-out scorer. We do NOT
   226:         # have the true tau, so we cannot (and do not) compute the metric here.
   227:         payload = base64.b64encode(
   228:             np.ascontiguousarray(tau_hat, dtype=np.float64).tobytes()
   229:         ).decode("ascii")
   230:         print(f"CATE_PRED dataset={args.dataset} seed={args.seed} rep={rep} "
   231:               f"data_seed={data_seed} n={tau_hat.shape[0]} tau_hat={payload}",
   232:               flush=True)
   233: 
   234:     print(f"Done {args.dataset}: emitted {args.n_reps} prediction sets in "
   235:           f"{time.time() - start:.1f}s", flush=True)
   236: 
   237: 
   238: if __name__ == "__main__":
   239:     main()

参考基线

以下是只读的参考实现。每一项都展示了一个强基线的可编辑区域是什么样子，并附有几行周边上下文以帮助定位。请研读它们，但要编写你自己的算法——逐字照搬某个基线将被检测出来，并按基线复现评分。

s_learner 基线 — 可编辑区域 [只读 — 参考实现]

在 scikit-learn/custom_cate.py 中：

python
Lines 84–113:
    81: # EDITABLE: Custom CATE Estimator
    82: # =====================================================================
    83: 
    84: class CATEEstimator(BaseCATEEstimator):
    85:     """S-Learner: single model approach to CATE estimation.
    86: 
    87:     Fits a single outcome model mu(X, T) on the combined data, then
    88:     estimates CATE as mu(X, 1) - mu(X, 0).
    89:     Uses GradientBoostingRegressor as the base learner for flexibility.
    90:     """
    91: 
    92:     def __init__(self):
    93:         self._seed = int(os.environ.get("SEED", "42"))
    94:         self._model = GradientBoostingRegressor(
    95:             n_estimators=200,
    96:             max_depth=4,
    97:             learning_rate=0.1,
    98:             min_samples_leaf=20,
    99:             subsample=0.8,
   100:             random_state=self._seed,
   101:         )
   102: 
   103:     def fit(self, X, T, Y):
   104:         n, p = X.shape
   105:         XT = np.column_stack([X, T.reshape(-1, 1)])
   106:         self._model.fit(XT, Y)
   107:         return self
   108: 
   109:     def predict(self, X):
   110:         n = X.shape[0]
   111:         X1 = np.column_stack([X, np.ones((n, 1))])
   112:         X0 = np.column_stack([X, np.zeros((n, 1))])
   113:         return self._model.predict(X1) - self._model.predict(X0)
   114: 
   115: 
   116: # =====================================================================

    81: # EDITABLE: Custom CATE Estimator
    82: # =====================================================================
    83: 
    84: class CATEEstimator(BaseCATEEstimator):
    85:     """T-Learner: two separate models for treated and control groups.
    86: 
    87:     Fits mu0(X) on control data and mu1(X) on treated data, then
    88:     estimates CATE as mu1(X) - mu0(X).
    89:     Uses GradientBoostingRegressor for both models.
    90:     """
    91: 
    92:     def __init__(self):
    93:         self._seed = int(os.environ.get("SEED", "42"))
    94:         self._model0 = GradientBoostingRegressor(
    95:             n_estimators=200,
    96:             max_depth=4,
    97:             learning_rate=0.1,
    98:             min_samples_leaf=20,
    99:             subsample=0.8,
   100:             random_state=self._seed,
   101:         )
   102:         self._model1 = GradientBoostingRegressor(
   103:             n_estimators=200,
   104:             max_depth=4,
   105:             learning_rate=0.1,
   106:             min_samples_leaf=20,
   107:             subsample=0.8,
   108:             random_state=self._seed + 1,
   109:         )
   110: 
   111:     def fit(self, X, T, Y):
   112:         mask0 = T == 0
   113:         mask1 = T == 1
   114:         self._model0.fit(X[mask0], Y[mask0])
   115:         self._model1.fit(X[mask1], Y[mask1])
   116:         return self
   117: 
   118:     def predict(self, X):
   119:         return self._model1.predict(X) - self._model0.predict(X)
   120: 
   121: 
   122: # =====================================================================

ipw 基线 — 可编辑区域 [只读 — 参考实现]

在 scikit-learn/custom_cate.py 中：

python
Lines 84–123:
    81: # EDITABLE: Custom CATE Estimator
    82: # =====================================================================
    83: 
    84: class CATEEstimator(BaseCATEEstimator):
    85:     """IPW-based CATE estimator with propensity score weighting.
    86: 
    87:     1. Estimate propensity score e(X) = P(T=1|X) via logistic regression.
    88:     2. Construct IPW pseudo-outcomes: Y_ipw = T*Y/e(X) - (1-T)*Y/(1-e(X)).
    89:     3. Fit a regression model on X -> Y_ipw for CATE estimation.
    90: 
    91:     Clips propensity scores to [0.05, 0.95] for stability.
    92:     """
    93: 
    94:     def __init__(self):
    95:         self._seed = int(os.environ.get("SEED", "42"))
    96:         self._prop_model = GradientBoostingClassifier(
    97:             n_estimators=200, max_depth=3, learning_rate=0.1,
    98:             min_samples_leaf=20, subsample=0.8, random_state=self._seed,
    99:         )
   100:         self._outcome_model = GradientBoostingRegressor(
   101:             n_estimators=200,
   102:             max_depth=4,
   103:             learning_rate=0.1,
   104:             min_samples_leaf=20,
   105:             subsample=0.8,
   106:             random_state=self._seed + 1,
   107:         )
   108: 
   109:     def fit(self, X, T, Y):
   110:         # Estimate propensity scores
   111:         self._prop_model.fit(X, T)
   112:         e_hat = self._prop_model.predict_proba(X)[:, 1]
   113:         e_hat = np.clip(e_hat, 0.05, 0.95)
   114: 
   115:         # IPW pseudo-outcomes
   116:         pseudo_outcome = T * Y / e_hat - (1 - T) * Y / (1 - e_hat)
   117: 
   118:         # Fit outcome model on pseudo-outcomes
   119:         self._outcome_model.fit(X, pseudo_outcome)
   120:         return self
   121: 
   122:     def predict(self, X):
   123:         return self._outcome_model.predict(X)
   124: 
   125: 
   126: # =====================================================================

causal_forest 基线 — 可编辑区域 [只读 — 参考实现]

在 scikit-learn/custom_cate.py 中：

python
Lines 84–165:
    81: # EDITABLE: Custom CATE Estimator
    82: # =====================================================================
    83: 
    84: class CATEEstimator(BaseCATEEstimator):
    85:     """Causal Forest (via econml CausalForestDML).
    86: 
    87:     Combines double machine learning (DML) for debiasing with
    88:     generalized random forests for heterogeneous effect estimation.
    89: 
    90:     Steps:
    91:     1. Cross-fit nuisance models: E[Y|X] and E[T|X]
    92:     2. Compute residuals: Y_res = Y - E[Y|X], T_res = T - E[T|X]
    93:     3. Fit a causal forest on residualized outcomes
    94: 
    95:     Falls back to a pure-sklearn implementation if econml is unavailable.
    96:     """
    97: 
    98:     def __init__(self):
    99:         self._seed = int(os.environ.get("SEED", "42"))
   100:         self._use_econml = True
   101:         try:
   102:             from econml.dml import CausalForestDML
   103:             self._cf = CausalForestDML(
   104:                 model_y=GradientBoostingRegressor(
   105:                     n_estimators=100, max_depth=3, learning_rate=0.1,
   106:                     min_samples_leaf=20, random_state=self._seed,
   107:                 ),
   108:                 model_t=GradientBoostingRegressor(
   109:                     n_estimators=100, max_depth=3, learning_rate=0.1,
   110:                     min_samples_leaf=20, random_state=self._seed + 1,
   111:                 ),
   112:                 n_estimators=500,
   113:                 min_samples_leaf=5,
   114:                 max_depth=None,
   115:                 honest=True,
   116:                 inference=False,
   117:                 random_state=self._seed + 2,
   118:                 cv=3,
   119:             )
   120:         except ImportError:
   121:             self._use_econml = False
   122:             # Fallback: manual residualization + random forest
   123:             self._model_y = GradientBoostingRegressor(
   124:                 n_estimators=200, max_depth=4, learning_rate=0.1,
   125:                 min_samples_leaf=20, random_state=self._seed,
   126:             )
   127:             self._model_t = GradientBoostingClassifier(
   128:                 n_estimators=200, max_depth=4, learning_rate=0.1,
   129:                 min_samples_leaf=20, random_state=self._seed + 1,
   130:             )
   131:             self._cate_model = RandomForestRegressor(
   132:                 n_estimators=500, min_samples_leaf=5,
   133:                 max_features="sqrt", random_state=self._seed + 2,
   134:             )
   135: 
   136:     def fit(self, X, T, Y):
   137:         if self._use_econml:
   138:             self._cf.fit(Y, T, X=X)
   139:         else:
   140:             # Manual DML: cross-fit residuals
   141:             kf = KFold(n_splits=3, shuffle=True, random_state=self._seed)
   142:             Y_res = np.zeros_like(Y)
   143:             T_res = np.zeros_like(T, dtype=float)
   144: 
   145:             for train_idx, val_idx in kf.split(X):
   146:                 my = clone(self._model_y).fit(X[train_idx], Y[train_idx])
   147:                 mt = clone(self._model_t).fit(X[train_idx], T[train_idx])
   148:                 Y_res[val_idx] = Y[val_idx] - my.predict(X[val_idx])
   149:                 T_res[val_idx] = T[val_idx] - mt.predict_proba(X[val_idx])[:, 1]
   150: 
   151:             # R-Learner-style pseudo-outcome with stable divisor + sample
   152:             # weighting so small |T_res| doesn't explode the fit.
   153:             safe_T = np.where(np.abs(T_res) > 0.01, T_res, np.sign(T_res) * 0.01 + 1e-8)
   154:             pseudo = Y_res / safe_T
   155:             weights = T_res ** 2
   156:             q = np.percentile(np.abs(pseudo), 95)
   157:             pseudo = np.clip(pseudo, -q, q)
   158:             self._cate_model.fit(X, pseudo, sample_weight=weights)
   159:         return self
   160: 
   161:     def predict(self, X):
   162:         if self._use_econml:
   163:             return self._cf.effect(X).flatten()
   164:         else:
   165:             return self._cate_model.predict(X)
   166: 
   167: 
   168: # =====================================================================

dr_learner 基线 — 可编辑区域 [只读 — 参考实现]

在 scikit-learn/custom_cate.py 中：

python
Lines 84–173:
    81: # EDITABLE: Custom CATE Estimator
    82: # =====================================================================
    83: 
    84: class CATEEstimator(BaseCATEEstimator):
    85:     """DR-Learner: Doubly Robust CATE estimation.
    86: 
    87:     Steps:
    88:     1. Cross-fit nuisance models:
    89:        - mu0(X) = E[Y|X, T=0], mu1(X) = E[Y|X, T=1]  (outcome models)
    90:        - e(X) = P(T=1|X)  (propensity score)
    91:     2. Compute doubly-robust pseudo-outcomes:
    92:        phi(X) = mu1(X) - mu0(X)
    93:               + T*(Y - mu1(X))/e(X)
    94:               - (1-T)*(Y - mu0(X))/(1-e(X))
    95:     3. Fit a final CATE model on X -> phi(X)
    96:     """
    97: 
    98:     def __init__(self):
    99:         self._seed = int(os.environ.get("SEED", "42"))
   100: 
   101:     def _make_model_y(self):
   102:         return GradientBoostingRegressor(
   103:             n_estimators=200, max_depth=4, learning_rate=0.1,
   104:             min_samples_leaf=20, subsample=0.8, random_state=self._seed,
   105:         )
   106: 
   107:     def _make_model_t(self):
   108:         return GradientBoostingClassifier(
   109:             n_estimators=200, max_depth=3, learning_rate=0.1,
   110:             min_samples_leaf=20, subsample=0.8, random_state=self._seed + 1,
   111:         )
   112: 
   113:     def _make_cate_model(self):
   114:         return GradientBoostingRegressor(
   115:             n_estimators=200, max_depth=3, learning_rate=0.05,
   116:             min_samples_leaf=20, subsample=0.8, random_state=self._seed + 2,
   117:         )
   118: 
   119:     def fit(self, X, T, Y):
   120:         n = len(Y)
   121: 
   122:         # Cross-fit nuisance models
   123:         kf = KFold(n_splits=5, shuffle=True, random_state=self._seed)
   124:         mu0_hat = np.zeros(n)
   125:         mu1_hat = np.zeros(n)
   126:         e_hat = np.zeros(n)
   127: 
   128:         for train_idx, val_idx in kf.split(X):
   129:             # Outcome models (separate for T=0 and T=1)
   130:             mask0_train = T[train_idx] == 0
   131:             mask1_train = T[train_idx] == 1
   132: 
   133:             m0 = self._make_model_y()
   134:             m1 = self._make_model_y()
   135: 
   136:             if mask0_train.sum() > 5:
   137:                 m0.fit(X[train_idx[mask0_train]], Y[train_idx[mask0_train]])
   138:                 mu0_hat[val_idx] = m0.predict(X[val_idx])
   139:             else:
   140:                 mu0_hat[val_idx] = Y[T == 0].mean() if (T == 0).sum() > 0 else Y.mean()
   141: 
   142:             if mask1_train.sum() > 5:
   143:                 m1.fit(X[train_idx[mask1_train]], Y[train_idx[mask1_train]])
   144:                 mu1_hat[val_idx] = m1.predict(X[val_idx])
   145:             else:
   146:                 mu1_hat[val_idx] = Y[T == 1].mean() if (T == 1).sum() > 0 else Y.mean()
   147: 
   148:             # Propensity model
   149:             mt = self._make_model_t()
   150:             mt.fit(X[train_idx], T[train_idx])
   151:             e_hat[val_idx] = mt.predict_proba(X[val_idx])[:, 1]
   152: 
   153:         # Clip propensity scores
   154:         e_hat = np.clip(e_hat, 0.05, 0.95)
   155: 
   156:         # Doubly-robust pseudo-outcomes
   157:         pseudo = (
   158:             mu1_hat - mu0_hat
   159:             + T * (Y - mu1_hat) / e_hat
   160:             - (1 - T) * (Y - mu0_hat) / (1 - e_hat)
   161:         )
   162: 
   163:         # Clip extreme pseudo-outcomes
   164:         q = np.percentile(np.abs(pseudo), 97)
   165:         pseudo = np.clip(pseudo, -q, q)
   166: 
   167:         # Fit CATE model on pseudo-outcomes
   168:         self._cate_model = self._make_cate_model()
   169:         self._cate_model.fit(X, pseudo)
   170:         return self
   171: 
   172:     def predict(self, X):
   173:         return self._cate_model.predict(X)
   174: 
   175: 
   176: # =====================================================================

r_learner 基线 — 可编辑区域 [只读 — 参考实现]

在 scikit-learn/custom_cate.py 中：

python
Lines 84–161:
    81: # EDITABLE: Custom CATE Estimator
    82: # =====================================================================
    83: 
    84: class CATEEstimator(BaseCATEEstimator):
    85:     """R-Learner: Robinson decomposition for CATE estimation.
    86: 
    87:     Based on the Robinson (1988) decomposition:
    88:         Y - m(X) = (T - e(X)) * tau(X) + epsilon
    89: 
    90:     Steps:
    91:     1. Cross-fit nuisance models:
    92:        - m(X) = E[Y|X]  (marginal outcome model)
    93:        - e(X) = P(T=1|X)  (propensity score)
    94:     2. Compute residuals: Y_tilde = Y - m(X), T_tilde = T - e(X)
    95:     3. Estimate tau(X) by minimizing: sum_i (Y_tilde_i - T_tilde_i * tau(X_i))^2
    96:        This is equivalent to weighted least squares with weight T_tilde^2.
    97:     """
    98: 
    99:     def __init__(self):
   100:         self._seed = int(os.environ.get("SEED", "42"))
   101: 
   102:     def _make_model_y(self):
   103:         return GradientBoostingRegressor(
   104:             n_estimators=200, max_depth=4, learning_rate=0.1,
   105:             min_samples_leaf=20, subsample=0.8, random_state=self._seed,
   106:         )
   107: 
   108:     def _make_model_t(self):
   109:         return GradientBoostingClassifier(
   110:             n_estimators=200, max_depth=3, learning_rate=0.1,
   111:             min_samples_leaf=20, subsample=0.8, random_state=self._seed + 1,
   112:         )
   113: 
   114:     def fit(self, X, T, Y):
   115:         n = len(Y)
   116: 
   117:         # Cross-fit nuisance models
   118:         kf = KFold(n_splits=5, shuffle=True, random_state=self._seed)
   119:         m_hat = np.zeros(n)
   120:         e_hat = np.zeros(n)
   121: 
   122:         for train_idx, val_idx in kf.split(X):
   123:             # Outcome model E[Y|X]
   124:             my = self._make_model_y()
   125:             my.fit(X[train_idx], Y[train_idx])
   126:             m_hat[val_idx] = my.predict(X[val_idx])
   127: 
   128:             # Propensity model P(T=1|X)
   129:             mt = self._make_model_t()
   130:             mt.fit(X[train_idx], T[train_idx])
   131:             e_hat[val_idx] = mt.predict_proba(X[val_idx])[:, 1]
   132: 
   133:         # Clip propensity scores
   134:         e_hat = np.clip(e_hat, 0.05, 0.95)
   135: 
   136:         # Residuals
   137:         Y_tilde = Y - m_hat
   138:         T_tilde = T - e_hat
   139: 
   140:         # R-Learner: pseudo-outcome = Y_tilde / T_tilde
   141:         # Weight = T_tilde^2 (higher weight where treatment variation is larger)
   142:         weights = T_tilde ** 2
   143:         # Avoid division by zero
   144:         safe_T = np.where(np.abs(T_tilde) > 0.01, T_tilde, np.sign(T_tilde) * 0.01 + 1e-8)
   145:         pseudo = Y_tilde / safe_T
   146: 
   147:         # Clip extreme pseudo-outcomes
   148:         q = np.percentile(np.abs(pseudo), 95)
   149:         pseudo = np.clip(pseudo, -q, q)
   150: 
   151:         # Weighted regression for CATE
   152:         # Use sample_weight = T_tilde^2 to prioritize informative samples
   153:         self._cate_model = GradientBoostingRegressor(
   154:             n_estimators=200, max_depth=3, learning_rate=0.05,
   155:             min_samples_leaf=20, subsample=0.8, random_state=self._seed + 2,
   156:         )
   157:         self._cate_model.fit(X, pseudo, sample_weight=weights)
   158:         return self
   159: 
   160:     def predict(self, X):
   161:         return self._cate_model.predict(X)
   162: 
   163: 
   164: # =====================================================================

提示

保持可编辑区域的函数/类签名完全一致；评估程序会按名称导入它们。
确定性很重要：随机种子已固定；不要引入隐藏的随机性。
上面的基线实现刻意做得很强。应力求算法层面的改进——况且许多超参数本来就锁定在可编辑范围之外。

时间预算

在提交之前，你有 5 小时的实际时间，涵盖你在此处所做的一切：阅读代码、编辑代码以及启动的任何试运行。

祝你好运。

🇬🇧 [English Original] Causal Treatment Effect

MLS-Bench: causal-treatment-effect

Causal Treatment Effect Estimation

Research Question

Design a novel estimator for Conditional Average Treatment Effects (CATE) from observational data that is accurate, robust to confounding, and generalizes across synthetic data-generating processes.

Background

Estimating heterogeneous treatment effects -- how the causal effect of a treatment varies across individuals -- is a core problem in causal inference. Given observational data with covariates X, binary treatment T, and outcome Y, the goal is to estimate tau(x) = E[Y(1) - Y(0) | X = x], the conditional average treatment effect.

Key challenges include:

Confounding: treatment assignment depends on covariates, so naive

comparisons are biased.
Heterogeneity: treatment effects vary across the covariate space in

complex, nonlinear ways.
Model misspecification: true response surfaces may not match parametric

assumptions.
Double robustness: ideally, the estimator is consistent if either the

outcome model or the propensity model is correct.

Classical approaches include S-Learner (single model), T-Learner (separate
outcome models per arm), and IPW (propensity reweighting). Modern methods use
orthogonalization or debiasing for better convergence rates: see Athey & Wager,
"Estimation and Inference of Heterogeneous Treatment Effects using Random
Forests," JASA 113(523), 2018 (arXiv:1510.04342); Kennedy, "Towards optimal
doubly robust estimation of heterogeneous causal effects," Electronic Journal
of Statistics 17(2), 2023 (arXiv:2004.14497); and Nie & Wager, "Quasi-Oracle
Estimation of Heterogeneous Treatment Effects," Biometrika 108(2), 2021
(arXiv:1712.04912).

Task

Modify the CATEEstimator class in custom_cate.py. The estimator must implement: 

python
class CATEEstimator:
    def fit(self, X, T, Y) -> "CATEEstimator":
        """Learn from observational covariates X, binary treatment T, outcome Y."""

    def predict(self, X):
        """Return predicted individual treatment effects tau_hat for each row of X."""

scikit-learn, numpy, and scipy are available.

Valid contributions may combine outcome modeling, propensity modeling,
orthogonalization, weighting, residualization, forests, neural models, or other
modular CATE ideas, as long as they address confounding and treatment-effect
heterogeneity.

   175:     """Load the pre-generated observational inputs for (dataset, data_seed).
   176: 
   177:     Only (X, T, Y) are available — the true treatment effect is held out by the
   178:     harness and is not present in this process.
   179:     """
   180:     path = os.path.join(_inputs_dir(), f"{dataset}_seed{data_seed}.npz.b64")
   181:     with open(path, "r") as f:
   182:         raw = base64.b64decode(f.read())
   183:     with np.load(io.BytesIO(raw)) as d:
   184:         return d["X"], d["T"], d["Y"]
   185: 
   186: 
   187: def cross_fit_predict(X, T, Y, n_splits, seed):
   188:     """Cross-fitted CATE prediction (same K-fold protocol as before).
   189: 
   190:     Fit on K-1 folds, predict held-out fold; aggregate to per-row tau_hat.
   191:     A fresh deep-copy of the estimator is used per fold (cross-fitting).
   192:     """
   193:     import copy
   194:     base = CATEEstimator()
   195:     kf = KFold(n_splits=n_splits, shuffle=True, random_state=seed)
   196:     tau_hat_all = np.zeros(len(X))
   197:     for train_idx, test_idx in kf.split(X):
   198:         est = copy.deepcopy(base)
   199:         est.fit(X[train_idx], T[train_idx], Y[train_idx])
   200:         tau_hat_all[test_idx] = est.predict(X[test_idx])
   201:     return tau_hat_all
   202: 
   203: 
   204: def main():
   205:     parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="CATE Estimation Benchmark")
   206:     parser.add_argument("--dataset", type=str, required=True,
   207:                         help="Opaque dataset token (the real dataset is held out)")
   208:     parser.add_argument("--seed", type=int, default=42,
   209:                         help="Base random seed (selects the pre-generated inputs)")
   210:     parser.add_argument("--n-splits", type=int, default=5,
   211:                         help="Number of cross-validation folds")
   212:     parser.add_argument("--n-reps", type=int, default=10,
   213:                         help="Number of repetitions with different data seeds")
   214:     args = parser.parse_args()
   215: 
   216:     print(f"Evaluating on {args.dataset} (seed={args.seed}, "
   217:           f"n_splits={args.n_splits}, n_reps={args.n_reps})", flush=True)
   218: 
   219:     start = time.time()
   220:     for rep in range(args.n_reps):
   221:         data_seed = args.seed + rep * 1000
   222:         X, T, Y = load_inputs(args.dataset, data_seed)
   223:         tau_hat = cross_fit_predict(X, T, Y, args.n_splits, data_seed)
   224: 
   225:         # Emit the cross-fitted predictions for the held-out scorer. We do NOT
   226:         # have the true tau, so we cannot (and do not) compute the metric here.
   227:         payload = base64.b64encode(
   228:             np.ascontiguousarray(tau_hat, dtype=np.float64).tobytes()
   229:         ).decode("ascii")
   230:         print(f"CATE_PRED dataset={args.dataset} seed={args.seed} rep={rep} "
   231:               f"data_seed={data_seed} n={tau_hat.shape[0]} tau_hat={payload}",
   232:               flush=True)
   233: 
   234:     print(f"Done {args.dataset}: emitted {args.n_reps} prediction sets in "
   235:           f"{time.time() - start:.1f}s", flush=True)
   236: 
   237: 
   238: if __name__ == "__main__":
   239:     main()

    81: # EDITABLE: Custom CATE Estimator
    82: # =====================================================================
    83: 
    84: class CATEEstimator(BaseCATEEstimator):
    85:     """S-Learner: single model approach to CATE estimation.
    86: 
    87:     Fits a single outcome model mu(X, T) on the combined data, then
    88:     estimates CATE as mu(X, 1) - mu(X, 0).
    89:     Uses GradientBoostingRegressor as the base learner for flexibility.
    90:     """
    91: 
    92:     def __init__(self):
    93:         self._seed = int(os.environ.get("SEED", "42"))
    94:         self._model = GradientBoostingRegressor(
    95:             n_estimators=200,
    96:             max_depth=4,
    97:             learning_rate=0.1,
    98:             min_samples_leaf=20,
    99:             subsample=0.8,
   100:             random_state=self._seed,
   101:         )
   102: 
   103:     def fit(self, X, T, Y):
   104:         n, p = X.shape
   105:         XT = np.column_stack([X, T.reshape(-1, 1)])
   106:         self._model.fit(XT, Y)
   107:         return self
   108: 
   109:     def predict(self, X):
   110:         n = X.shape[0]
   111:         X1 = np.column_stack([X, np.ones((n, 1))])
   112:         X0 = np.column_stack([X, np.zeros((n, 1))])
   113:         return self._model.predict(X1) - self._model.predict(X0)
   114: 
   115: 
   116: # =====================================================================

t_learner baseline — editable region [READ-ONLY — reference implementation]

In scikit-learn/custom_cate.py:

Lines 84–119:
    81: # EDITABLE: Custom CATE Estimator
    82: # =====================================================================
    83: 
    84: class CATEEstimator(BaseCATEEstimator):
    85:     """T-Learner: two separate models for treated and control groups.
    86: 
    87:     Fits mu0(X) on control data and mu1(X) on treated data, then
    88:     estimates CATE as mu1(X) - mu0(X).
    89:     Uses GradientBoostingRegressor for both models.
    90:     """
    91: 
    92:     def __init__(self):
    93:         self._seed = int(os.environ.get("SEED", "42"))
    94:         self._model0 = GradientBoostingRegressor(
    95:             n_estimators=200,
    96:             max_depth=4,
    97:             learning_rate=0.1,
    98:             min_samples_leaf=20,
    99:             subsample=0.8,
   100:             random_state=self._seed,
   101:         )
   102:         self._model1 = GradientBoostingRegressor(
   103:             n_estimators=200,
   104:             max_depth=4,
   105:             learning_rate=0.1,
   106:             min_samples_leaf=20,
   107:             subsample=0.8,
   108:             random_state=self._seed + 1,
   109:         )
   110: 
   111:     def fit(self, X, T, Y):
   112:         mask0 = T == 0
   113:         mask1 = T == 1
   114:         self._model0.fit(X[mask0], Y[mask0])
   115:         self._model1.fit(X[mask1], Y[mask1])
   116:         return self
   117: 
   118:     def predict(self, X):
   119:         return self._model1.predict(X) - self._model0.predict(X)
   120: 
   121: 
   122: # =====================================================================

ipw baseline — editable region [READ-ONLY — reference implementation]

In scikit-learn/custom_cate.py:

python
Lines 84–123:
    81: # EDITABLE: Custom CATE Estimator
    82: # =====================================================================
    83: 
    84: class CATEEstimator(BaseCATEEstimator):
    85:     """IPW-based CATE estimator with propensity score weighting.
    86: 
    87:     1. Estimate propensity score e(X) = P(T=1|X) via logistic regression.
    88:     2. Construct IPW pseudo-outcomes: Y_ipw = T*Y/e(X) - (1-T)*Y/(1-e(X)).
    89:     3. Fit a regression model on X -> Y_ipw for CATE estimation.
    90: 
    91:     Clips propensity scores to [0.05, 0.95] for stability.
    92:     """
    93: 
    94:     def __init__(self):
    95:         self._seed = int(os.environ.get("SEED", "42"))
    96:         self._prop_model = GradientBoostingClassifier(
    97:             n_estimators=200, max_depth=3, learning_rate=0.1,
    98:             min_samples_leaf=20, subsample=0.8, random_state=self._seed,
    99:         )
   100:         self._outcome_model = GradientBoostingRegressor(
   101:             n_estimators=200,
   102:             max_depth=4,
   103:             learning_rate=0.1,
   104:             min_samples_leaf=20,
   105:             subsample=0.8,
   106:             random_state=self._seed + 1,
   107:         )
   108: 
   109:     def fit(self, X, T, Y):
   110:         # Estimate propensity scores
   111:         self._prop_model.fit(X, T)
   112:         e_hat = self._prop_model.predict_proba(X)[:, 1]
   113:         e_hat = np.clip(e_hat, 0.05, 0.95)
   114: 
   115:         # IPW pseudo-outcomes
   116:         pseudo_outcome = T * Y / e_hat - (1 - T) * Y / (1 - e_hat)
   117: 
   118:         # Fit outcome model on pseudo-outcomes
   119:         self._outcome_model.fit(X, pseudo_outcome)
   120:         return self
   121: 
   122:     def predict(self, X):
   123:         return self._outcome_model.predict(X)
   124: 
   125: 
   126: # =====================================================================

causal_forest baseline — editable region [READ-ONLY — reference implementation]

Lines 84–165:
    81: # EDITABLE: Custom CATE Estimator
    82: # =====================================================================
    83: 
    84: class CATEEstimator(BaseCATEEstimator):
    85:     """Causal Forest (via econml CausalForestDML).
    86: 
    87:     Combines double machine learning (DML) for debiasing with
    88:     generalized random forests for heterogeneous effect estimation.
    89: 
    90:     Steps:
    91:     1. Cross-fit nuisance models: E[Y|X] and E[T|X]
    92:     2. Compute residuals: Y_res = Y - E[Y|X], T_res = T - E[T|X]
    93:     3. Fit a causal forest on residualized outcomes
    94: 
    95:     Falls back to a pure-sklearn implementation if econml is unavailable.
    96:     """
    97: 
    98:     def __init__(self):
    99:         self._seed = int(os.environ.get("SEED", "42"))
   100:         self._use_econml = True
   101:         try:
   102:             from econml.dml import CausalForestDML
   103:             self._cf = CausalForestDML(
   104:                 model_y=GradientBoostingRegressor(
   105:                     n_estimators=100, max_depth=3, learning_rate=0.1,
   106:                     min_samples_leaf=20, random_state=self._seed,
   107:                 ),
   108:                 model_t=GradientBoostingRegressor(
   109:                     n_estimators=100, max_depth=3, learning_rate=0.1,
   110:                     min_samples_leaf=20, random_state=self._seed + 1,
   111:                 ),
   112:                 n_estimators=500,
   113:                 min_samples_leaf=5,
   114:                 max_depth=None,
   115:                 honest=True,
   116:                 inference=False,
   117:                 random_state=self._seed + 2,
   118:                 cv=3,
   119:             )
   120:         except ImportError:
   121:             self._use_econml = False
   122:             # Fallback: manual residualization + random forest
   123:             self._model_y = GradientBoostingRegressor(
   124:                 n_estimators=200, max_depth=4, learning_rate=0.1,
   125:                 min_samples_leaf=20, random_state=self._seed,
   126:             )
   127:             self._model_t = GradientBoostingClassifier(
   128:                 n_estimators=200, max_depth=4, learning_rate=0.1,
   129:                 min_samples_leaf=20, random_state=self._seed + 1,
   130:             )
   131:             self._cate_model = RandomForestRegressor(
   132:                 n_estimators=500, min_samples_leaf=5,
   133:                 max_features="sqrt", random_state=self._seed + 2,
   134:             )
   135: 
   136:     def fit(self, X, T, Y):
   137:         if self._use_econml:
   138:             self._cf.fit(Y, T, X=X)
   139:         else:
   140:             # Manual DML: cross-fit residuals
   141:             kf = KFold(n_splits=3, shuffle=True, random_state=self._seed)
   142:             Y_res = np.zeros_like(Y)
   143:             T_res = np.zeros_like(T, dtype=float)
   144: 
   145:             for train_idx, val_idx in kf.split(X):
   146:                 my = clone(self._model_y).fit(X[train_idx], Y[train_idx])
   147:                 mt = clone(self._model_t).fit(X[train_idx], T[train_idx])
   148:                 Y_res[val_idx] = Y[val_idx] - my.predict(X[val_idx])
   149:                 T_res[val_idx] = T[val_idx] - mt.predict_proba(X[val_idx])[:, 1]
   150: 
   151:             # R-Learner-style pseudo-outcome with stable divisor + sample
   152:             # weighting so small |T_res| doesn't explode the fit.
   153:             safe_T = np.where(np.abs(T_res) > 0.01, T_res, np.sign(T_res) * 0.01 + 1e-8)
   154:             pseudo = Y_res / safe_T
   155:             weights = T_res ** 2
   156:             q = np.percentile(np.abs(pseudo), 95)
   157:             pseudo = np.clip(pseudo, -q, q)
   158:             self._cate_model.fit(X, pseudo, sample_weight=weights)
   159:         return self
   160: 
   161:     def predict(self, X):
   162:         if self._use_econml:
   163:             return self._cf.effect(X).flatten()
   164:         else:
   165:             return self._cate_model.predict(X)
   166: 
   167: 
   168: # =====================================================================

dr_learner baseline — editable region [READ-ONLY — reference implementation]

In scikit-learn/custom_cate.py:

python
Lines 84–173:
    81: # EDITABLE: Custom CATE Estimator
    82: # =====================================================================
    83: 
    84: class CATEEstimator(BaseCATEEstimator):
    85:     """DR-Learner: Doubly Robust CATE estimation.
    86: 
    87:     Steps:
    88:     1. Cross-fit nuisance models:
    89:        - mu0(X) = E[Y|X, T=0], mu1(X) = E[Y|X, T=1]  (outcome models)
    90:        - e(X) = P(T=1|X)  (propensity score)
    91:     2. Compute doubly-robust pseudo-outcomes:
    92:        phi(X) = mu1(X) - mu0(X)
    93:               + T*(Y - mu1(X))/e(X)
    94:               - (1-T)*(Y - mu0(X))/(1-e(X))
    95:     3. Fit a final CATE model on X -> phi(X)
    96:     """
    97: 
    98:     def __init__(self):
    99:         self._seed = int(os.environ.get("SEED", "42"))
   100: 
   101:     def _make_model_y(self):
   102:         return GradientBoostingRegressor(
   103:             n_estimators=200, max_depth=4, learning_rate=0.1,
   104:             min_samples_leaf=20, subsample=0.8, random_state=self._seed,
   105:         )
   106: 
   107:     def _make_model_t(self):
   108:         return GradientBoostingClassifier(
   109:             n_estimators=200, max_depth=3, learning_rate=0.1,
   110:             min_samples_leaf=20, subsample=0.8, random_state=self._seed + 1,
   111:         )
   112: 
   113:     def _make_cate_model(self):
   114:         return GradientBoostingRegressor(
   115:             n_estimators=200, max_depth=3, learning_rate=0.05,
   116:             min_samples_leaf=20, subsample=0.8, random_state=self._seed + 2,
   117:         )
   118: 
   119:     def fit(self, X, T, Y):
   120:         n = len(Y)
   121: 
   122:         # Cross-fit nuisance models
   123:         kf = KFold(n_splits=5, shuffle=True, random_state=self._seed)
   124:         mu0_hat = np.zeros(n)
   125:         mu1_hat = np.zeros(n)
   126:         e_hat = np.zeros(n)
   127: 
   128:         for train_idx, val_idx in kf.split(X):
   129:             # Outcome models (separate for T=0 and T=1)
   130:             mask0_train = T[train_idx] == 0
   131:             mask1_train = T[train_idx] == 1
   132: 
   133:             m0 = self._make_model_y()
   134:             m1 = self._make_model_y()
   135: 
   136:             if mask0_train.sum() > 5:
   137:                 m0.fit(X[train_idx[mask0_train]], Y[train_idx[mask0_train]])
   138:                 mu0_hat[val_idx] = m0.predict(X[val_idx])
   139:             else:
   140:                 mu0_hat[val_idx] = Y[T == 0].mean() if (T == 0).sum() > 0 else Y.mean()
   141: 
   142:             if mask1_train.sum() > 5:
   143:                 m1.fit(X[train_idx[mask1_train]], Y[train_idx[mask1_train]])
   144:                 mu1_hat[val_idx] = m1.predict(X[val_idx])
   145:             else:
   146:                 mu1_hat[val_idx] = Y[T == 1].mean() if (T == 1).sum() > 0 else Y.mean()
   147: 
   148:             # Propensity model
   149:             mt = self._make_model_t()
   150:             mt.fit(X[train_idx], T[train_idx])
   151:             e_hat[val_idx] = mt.predict_proba(X[val_idx])[:, 1]
   152: 
   153:         # Clip propensity scores
   154:         e_hat = np.clip(e_hat, 0.05, 0.95)
   155: 
   156:         # Doubly-robust pseudo-outcomes
   157:         pseudo = (
   158:             mu1_hat - mu0_hat
   159:             + T * (Y - mu1_hat) / e_hat
   160:             - (1 - T) * (Y - mu0_hat) / (1 - e_hat)
   161:         )
   162: 
   163:         # Clip extreme pseudo-outcomes
   164:         q = np.percentile(np.abs(pseudo), 97)
   165:         pseudo = np.clip(pseudo, -q, q)
   166: 
   167:         # Fit CATE model on pseudo-outcomes
   168:         self._cate_model = self._make_cate_model()
   169:         self._cate_model.fit(X, pseudo)
   170:         return self
   171: 
   172:     def predict(self, X):
   173:         return self._cate_model.predict(X)
   174: 
   175: 
   176: # =====================================================================

r_learner baseline — editable region [READ-ONLY — reference implementation]

In scikit-learn/custom_cate.py:

python
Lines 84–161:
    81: # EDITABLE: Custom CATE Estimator
    82: # =====================================================================
    83: 
    84: class CATEEstimator(BaseCATEEstimator):
    85:     """R-Learner: Robinson decomposition for CATE estimation.
    86: 
    87:     Based on the Robinson (1988) decomposition:
    88:         Y - m(X) = (T - e(X)) * tau(X) + epsilon
    89: 
    90:     Steps:
    91:     1. Cross-fit nuisance models:
    92:        - m(X) = E[Y|X]  (marginal outcome model)
    93:        - e(X) = P(T=1|X)  (propensity score)
    94:     2. Compute residuals: Y_tilde = Y - m(X), T_tilde = T - e(X)
    95:     3. Estimate tau(X) by minimizing: sum_i (Y_tilde_i - T_tilde_i * tau(X_i))^2
    96:        This is equivalent to weighted least squares with weight T_tilde^2.
    97:     """
    98: 
    99:     def __init__(self):
   100:         self._seed = int(os.environ.get("SEED", "42"))
   101: 
   102:     def _make_model_y(self):
   103:         return GradientBoostingRegressor(
   104:             n_estimators=200, max_depth=4, learning_rate=0.1,
   105:             min_samples_leaf=20, subsample=0.8, random_state=self._seed,
   106:         )
   107: 
   108:     def _make_model_t(self):
   109:         return GradientBoostingClassifier(
   110:             n_estimators=200, max_depth=3, learning_rate=0.1,
   111:             min_samples_leaf=20, subsample=0.8, random_state=self._seed + 1,
   112:         )
   113: 
   114:     def fit(self, X, T, Y):
   115:         n = len(Y)
   116: 
   117:         # Cross-fit nuisance models
   118:         kf = KFold(n_splits=5, shuffle=True, random_state=self._seed)
   119:         m_hat = np.zeros(n)
   120:         e_hat = np.zeros(n)
   121: 
   122:         for train_idx, val_idx in kf.split(X):
   123:             # Outcome model E[Y|X]
   124:             my = self._make_model_y()
   125:             my.fit(X[train_idx], Y[train_idx])
   126:             m_hat[val_idx] = my.predict(X[val_idx])
   127: 
   128:             # Propensity model P(T=1|X)
   129:             mt = self._make_model_t()
   130:             mt.fit(X[train_idx], T[train_idx])
   131:             e_hat[val_idx] = mt.predict_proba(X[val_idx])[:, 1]
   132: 
   133:         # Clip propensity scores
   134:         e_hat = np.clip(e_hat, 0.05, 0.95)
   135: 
   136:         # Residuals
   137:         Y_tilde = Y - m_hat
   138:         T_tilde = T - e_hat
   139: 
   140:         # R-Learner: pseudo-outcome = Y_tilde / T_tilde
   141:         # Weight = T_tilde^2 (higher weight where treatment variation is larger)
   142:         weights = T_tilde ** 2
   143:         # Avoid division by zero
   144:         safe_T = np.where(np.abs(T_tilde) > 0.01, T_tilde, np.sign(T_tilde) * 0.01 + 1e-8)
   145:         pseudo = Y_tilde / safe_T
   146: 
   147:         # Clip extreme pseudo-outcomes
   148:         q = np.percentile(np.abs(pseudo), 95)
   149:         pseudo = np.clip(pseudo, -q, q)
   150: 
   151:         # Weighted regression for CATE
   152:         # Use sample_weight = T_tilde^2 to prioritize informative samples
   153:         self._cate_model = GradientBoostingRegressor(
   154:             n_estimators=200, max_depth=3, learning_rate=0.05,
   155:             min_samples_leaf=20, subsample=0.8, random_state=self._seed + 2,
   156:         )
   157:         self._cate_model.fit(X, pseudo, sample_weight=weights)
   158:         return self
   159: 
   160:     def predict(self, X):
   161:         return self._cate_model.predict(X)
   162: 
   163: 
   164: # =====================================================================

Tips

Keep the function/class signatures of the editable regions identical;
evaluation imports them by name.
Determinism matters: seeds are fixed; don't introduce hidden randomness.
The baseline implementations above are deliberately strong. Aim for an
algorithmic improvement — many hyperparameters are locked outside the editable surface anyway.

Time Budget

You have 5 hours of wall-clock time before submission, covering
everything you do here: reading the code, editing it, and any trial runs
you launch.

Good luck.

09 本地 autoresearch：树探索与判断题

树数据库已收敛 任务: nanogpt_short 多集群 worker：360-1 / 360-2 / ophis-gpu
这不是 MLS-Bench 的三个长训 agent，而是另一条独立实验线：每个节点是一个显式 nanoGPT 短训 setting，worker 实测最终 val_loss，master/perturb/researcher 再从已有节点生成新的 setting，最后从树中构造排序判断题。所有节点和结果仍保留在 autoresearch/tree.db 与 autoresearch/results/。

图 9-A：树节点的 val_loss 分布

9.2 树探索的中间产物与观测闭环

显式 setting→ 短训 worker→ val_loss + loss curve→ researcher / expansion / perturb→ 新节点

一个节点不只是“模型猜了一个分数”：setting_json 记录模型深度/宽度、学习率、 beta、weight decay、warmup、batch size、steps 等；metrics_json 记录短训的实测 loss；result_dir 指向该次运行目录。这样后面的候选生成和 judge 题目都能沿 parent 链回看实际探索历史。

展开：不要把三个 autoresearch 汇总文件混为同一个快照
文件统计登记含义
results/run_report.jsondone 2446 / failed 58 / pending 256 / running 36；这是早期运行快照只能作为历史进度，不能覆盖当前 DB
results/progress_1786770302.jsontotal 2655；done 2297；同样是 8/15 快照保留用于追踪树如何扩大
results/final_report.json360 道题：phase_cd 210 + calibration 150对应这份 final judge 汇总，不等于 sibling pilot
experiments/tree_sibling_judge/results/summary.json5 个模型 × type1 50 + type2 50；同一批 sibling 题独立的树状兄弟节点题实验

9.3 判断题结果：按数据源分别登记

分数定义：raw = 1 - inversions / C(n,2)；norm = (raw - 0.5) / 0.5。 因为题池、候选 spread、模型集合不同，下面几张表不能横向拼成一个 leaderboard。

9.4 “observable” 变量在这里到底是什么

Type1 给 anchor 的 setting + 实测最终 val_loss + 候选 setting； Type2 在此基础上再给 anchor 的 24 点 validation loss curve。 因此 Type2 的增量不是“多给一个最终答案”，而是多给训练过程中的轨迹。 另外，pilot 还测试了把 3 层祖先的 setting + val_loss 加进 prompt；这是 exploration history observable，与 loss curve 是两种不同的中间信息。
展开：sibling pilot 的 context / shuffled / both 结果（原始 summary.json）
模型baseshuffledcontextboth
gpt-5.6-sol+0.184+0.144+0.264+0.276
gpt-5.5+0.100+0.120+0.248+0.220
gemini-3.6-flash+0.032+0.036+0.144+0.148
qwen3.7-max+0.008+0.120+0.164+0.092
claude-opus-5−0.092−0.040+0.112+0.088

解释：base 没有祖先上下文；shuffled 只打乱选项顺序；context 加 3 层祖先；both 同时做两件事。每格 n=50。

11 Robotics / JEPA 四任务（eb_jepa + CleanDiffuser）

jepa-planning 已完成 autoresearch 其余三任务仅链路验证 机器: ophis-gpu（8×H100） 上游: facebookresearch/eb_jepa · CleanDiffuserTeam/CleanDiffuser
本节口径：四个 Robotics 任务里，只有 jepa-planning 跑了完整的 MLS-Bench InteractiveAgent autoresearch（3 个并行实例）；其余三个任务 （prediction-loss / regularizer / diffusion-guidance）只完成了「环境 + 数据 + 训练管线」的 链路验证，没有任何 agent 分数。下文严格区分这两类信息。

11.1 四个任务是什么、改什么、看什么指标

📊 §11.1 四任务定义表（点击折叠）
任务研究问题agent 可改组件（editable）指标方向官方 baseline我们的状态
jepa-planning
latent world-model planner		 goal-conditioned 规划如何利用固定 latent world model 提升导航成功率（Two Rooms 迷宫） planner 采样/更新逻辑（CEM/MPPI/iCEM 家族），world model 冻结不可改 success_rate ↑ · mean_dist ↓ · steps_to_success ↓（horizon 30/60/90 三档） random .55/.70/.70；CEM .70/.85/.95；iCEM .80/.90/.85；MPPI .75/.95/.95 ✅ 3 实例完成，最佳 0.75/0.95/0.95
jepa-prediction-loss
temporal latent prediction loss		 latent 预测目标函数（MSE vs Smooth L1 vs Cosine）如何影响多步视频表征质量（Moving MNIST 检测 AP） 预测 loss 函数形式 detection AP ↑（small henc=16 / base 32 / large 64 三档） MSE .602/.651/.641；SmoothL1 .616/.640/.648；Cosine .561/.548/.466 🔶 仅链路验证（完整 Moving MNIST 781MB 加载 + small 缩短版 1 epoch 出 AP 正常）
jepa-regularizer
anti-collapse regularizer		 自监督正则（VICReg/SigReg/Barlow Twins）如何防止表征坍缩、提升线性探针精度 防坍缩正则项 linear-probe acc ↑（ResNet-18/34/50 探针三档） naive 14.6/13.8/17.3；VICReg 89.9/89.5/91.4；SigReg 90.5/91.0/91.2；Barlow 89.1/90.3/90.6 🔶 仅环境就绪，未跑训练
robo-diffusion-guidance
diffusion trajectory planner		 固定轨迹级 diffusion planner 上哪种 guidance 最优（D4RL MuJoCo 三环境） guidance 机制（classifier guidance / CFG / 无 guidance 等） D4RL normalized score ↑（hopper/walker2d/halfcheetah medium-v2） default .962/.766/.436；CFG .921/.762/.413；no_guid .436/.602/.412；DD .935/.697/.435 🔶 仅链路验证（D4RL 加载+训练+mujoco rollout 200 步通过，score=0.0087 只证明管线通）

图 11-A：Two Rooms 三个 horizon 的 success rate（黄柱 = agent 两实例最好值）
h30 组：random / CEM / iCEM / MPPI / agent 最佳h60 组同序h90 组：random / CEM / iCEM / MPPI（agent .95 与 MPPI 并列）黄色柱 = agent
单次评测 ±0.05~0.1 属随机波动范围：agent 的 h30 0.75 vs iCEM 0.80 不构成显著结论；两实例方案均属 hybrid CEM/iCEM 家族。

11.3 三个实例的完整 trajectory（日志原文摘录）

🧪 实例 A（v3_1）：首测即高峰，预算管理失败案例
Step 1-3: agent 阅读 instruction.md，确认 editable 组件为 planner（world model 冻结）。 决定实现 CustomPlanner。 Step 4: 写入 custom_planner.py —— hybrid CEM+iCEM： - elite 复用（跨迭代保留 top-k 精英并加噪声扰动） - 动量项（均值漂移惯性） - 时序平滑（相邻 timestep 动作序列相关性） 初始超参: num_samples=64, elites_size=max(10, num_samples//10), var_scale=1.8 Step 5: test#1 → 跑 horizon-30/60/90 三档评测 [TEST] horizon-30: success_rate=0.75 mean_dist=7.92 elapsed=332s [TEST] horizon-60: success_rate=0.85 mean_dist=4.75 elapsed=417s [TEST] horizon-90: success_rate=0.90 mean_dist=4.26 elapsed=668s → 首次 test 即超过 CEM/random，接近 MPPI。 Step 6-8: agent 尝试继续改进，连续三次 edit 工具调用失败： ERROR: Invalid arguments for 'edit': WorkspaceTools.edit() missing 1 required positional argument: 'op' 另有 conda run horizon_30.sh / horizon_60.sh 执行失败记录。 → 剩余 step 预算被烧光，无法再产出新版本。 Step 9: 只能原样提交 test#1 结果 submit(1)。 最终 leaderboard 行: deepseek-chat is_final=true 0.75/0.85/0.90 (332/417/668s) 教训：预算管理本身是 autoresearch 能力的一部分——工具调用格式错误连续烧掉 全部剩余 step，导致拿到高分也无法继续优化。
🧪 实例 B（v3_2）：主动权衡，最终提交追平最强 baseline
Step 1: 同样实现 CustomPlanner（hybrid CEM+iCEM），核心机制与实例 A 类似但独立调参： - elites_size=max(10, num_samples//10) - var_scale=1.8（采样方差放大） - 动量 + 时序平滑 Step 2: test#1 → 0.85(h30) / 0.90(h60) / 0.85(h90) 注意：h30 比 A 高（0.85 vs 0.75），但 h90 只有 0.85。 Step 3-4: agent 分析各 horizon 表现后重写 planner（约 190→230 行）， 重点加强长 horizon 的规划稳定性（更保守的方差调度 + 更强的精英保留）。 Step 5+: 后续 test 达到 0.75(h30) / 0.95(h60) / 0.95(h90)，elapsed 852/852/1046s。 agent 主动权衡：接受 h30 从 0.85 降到 0.75，换取 h60/h90 双 0.95。 最终 submit 该版本。 leaderboard 最终行: 2026-08-19T11:45:48+00:00,deepseek-chat,true,42,0.75,7.8933,21.33,0.95,3.3238,32.42,0.95,1.9261,39.37,852,852,1046 亮点：h90 mean_dist=1.93 为全场最低（MPPI 3.28、CEM 3.44）； h60/h90 双 0.95 追平综合最强的 MPPI baseline。
🧪 实例 C（最早实例）：checkpoint 缺失触发本地重训，22 小时超时被杀
启动后 agent 发现 checkpoint ac_jepa_e11.pth.tar 不在 /data/eb_jepa/checkpoints/， 决定本地重新训练 world model： ~26 s/it × 260 it/epoch × 12 epoch ≈ 22 小时 远超单次运行超时窗口，进程被 SIGTERM 杀死（exit=143），无任何分数写入 leaderboard。 该实例没有 leaderboard 行，但它解释了「同样的代码有时 5 分钟出分、有时跑不完」的现象： 取决于 checkpoint 是否已缓存。后续两个实例（A/B）都直接复用了已有 checkpoint。
11.4 不要把其余三个任务当成已有结果：prediction-loss / regularizer / diffusion-guidance 目前只有「能跑通」的证据（数据加载、训练一步、评测管线输出正常）， leaderboard 里只有官方 baseline 行，没有任何 agent 提交。若要纳入对比， 需先在这三个任务上跑完整 autoresearch。
